Abstract (英文): Human Motion Prediction (HMP) aims to predict future human poses at different moments according to observed past motion sequences. Previous approaches mainly treated the prediction of different temporal moments as a single prediction task and learned the predictions of varied moments simultaneously, which would encounter a main limitation: the learning of short-term predictions (referring to ``near-future'' prediction) could be hindered by the predictions of long-term (referring to ``far-future'' prediction) motions. In this paper, we develop a novel temporal continual learning framework called Continual Prior Compensation (CPC) to progressively train HMP models, in which we divide the prediction task of motions corresponding to varied temporal moments into several subtasks and train the model in a multi-stage manner. To mitigate the prior information forgetting in the progressive training, we further introduce a learnable random variable Prior Compensation Factor (PCF) to explicitly measure the prior knowledge loss. We theoretically show that the PCF can be efficiently learned together with the model parameters by minimizing a reasonable upper bound of the objective function. The proposed CPC is further enhanced to estimate the prior information loss for each subtask and a new framework called Continual Prior Compensation++ (CPC++) with Fine-Grained Prior Compensation Factor (FGPCF) is finally developed. Our CPC and CPC++ frameworks are quite flexible and can be easily integrated with different HMP backbone models and adapted to various datasets and applications. Extensive experiments on three HMP benchmark datasets using multiple SOTA HMP backbones (PGBIG, siMLPe, MotionMixer, and LTD) demonstrate the effectiveness and flexibility of our frameworks.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Diffusion models for temporal prediction typically corrupt each future frame with independent noise, leaving temporal dependence entirely to the denoiser. We propose Dynamical State-Space Diffusion (DSSD), which learns the temporal structure of the corruption process itself. DSSD builds a temporal kernel from the damped oscillatory modes of a stable diagonal state-space model, whose learned decay rates and frequencies let the corruption capture multiple timescales and oscillatory correlations, while the denoiser architecture remains unchanged. Running this model on a finite clip, however, requires the state before the first frame, which is unobserved; starting from a zero state leaves the early frames with less noise than the diffusion schedule prescribes. We avoid this by using the Cholesky factor of the model's stationary covariance as the temporal operator. We prove that this operator is causal, exactly invertible, keeps each frame's noise variance equal to that of standard diffusion, and reduces to the identity at the clean end of the process, and that it is exactly the Kalman innovations form of the state-space model. The denoiser is trained to predict the temporally correlated noise, and sampling removes and reapplies the temporal mixing at each step. On scientific-field and robot-video datasets, DSSD outperforms diffusion baselines that share its denoiser, and the evolution of its learned modes during training shows that the temporal operator adapts to each dataset.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Automatic cardiac motion tracking from cine cardiac magnetic resonance (CMR) imaging is essential for quantifying myocardial motion abnormalities associated with cardiovascular disease. Existing methods fall into two groups: those that represent motion without anatomical correspondence across subjects and therefore limit inter-subject motion assessment; and those that represent motion with globally corresponding points, which typically require a time-consuming and error-prone semi-automatic step to fit a cardiac atlas to the subject space. To address these limitations, this study proposes CardioDrift, a fully automatic, atlas-based probabilistic framework that represents subject-specific cardiac motion on a 3D atlas mesh with globally corresponding points. The Coherent Point Drift (CPD) formulation is generalised to jointly register the atlas surfaces of multiple cardiac regions to the anatomically corresponding contours extracted from cine CMR segmentation maps throughout the cardiac cycle. Slice-shift correction is embedded in the same probabilistic model, estimating slice-wise translations that suppress the misalignment artefact limiting atlas-fitting accuracy. Evaluation on 1301 randomly selected UK Biobank subjects and the M&M dataset, CardioDrift attains Dice similarity coefficients (DSCs) of $92.23\pm8.70\%$, $82.11\pm7.56\%$, and $88.69\pm4.13\%$ for the left ventricle (LV), myocardium (Myo), and right ventricle (RV), exceeding the strongest baseline on every distance and overlap metric of both datasets.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Online reasoning video segmentation (Online RVS) requires a model to segment query-referred objects as a video streams in, using only the frames observed so far. Existing formulations largely assume a fixed target set throughout the video, overlooking a fundamental property of streaming reasoning: as new events occur, the objects that satisfy a query may change over time. To address this, we propose a new task, Generalized Reasoning Video Segmentation (GRVS), where the target set depends on query-relevant events accumulated over the observed video history. As new evidence accumulates, the target set can evolve from empty to one or multiple objects, requiring a model to continuously maintain target validity while localizing the valid objects currently in view. To evaluate GRVS, we build Isekai, a diagnostic benchmark of 60 videos and 197 queries across 11 temporal query families, with 12,569 scoring points whose target sets are causally annotated over time. We further propose Sekai, an online framework with two complementary components: an Event Memory Reasoner (EMR) that maintains query-relevant events and evolving target states in structured memory, and a Memory-Grounded Decoder (MGD) that identifies valid targets from this memory and localizes them in the current frame. Experiments show that Sekai significantly outperforms existing online RVS methods, achieving 50.33 J&F and improving over the strongest baseline by 3.2 points. Code, example annotations, and evaluation scripts are available at https://github.com/zenastangbi/Sekai.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Deep learning methods for pathology are constrained by the limited availability and high cost of large-scale pixel-level annotations. Synthetic data can alleviate this limitation, but existing pipelines often lack reliable quality control and remain weakly connected to downstream analysis. To address these issues, we propose a three-stage framework that integrates automated data screening, generative representation learning, and efficient adaptation to downstream tasks. First, we develop a pathology agent system to assess image quality and filter samples, resulting in TCGA-200K, a dataset containing 217,030 pathology patches selected from the public TCGA repository. Second, we train a generative model on the screened data. In addition to image synthesis, its backbone learns structural and semantic representations that can be transferred to downstream tasks. Third, we adapt the pretrained generative backbone to semantic segmentation using a lightweight task-specific head. We further introduce PathSeg-380K, a semantic segmentation dataset with expert annotations for approximately 380,000 cells across 12 cell types. Experiments on multiple pathology datasets show that the proposed framework achieves state-of-the-art segmentation performance. Our code is publicly available at https://github.com/script-Yang/Agent-GenPath.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Streaming video understanding requires models to repeatedly decide whether to respond or continue observing. Recently, Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) has been applied to this setting, with existing approaches emphasizing reward design while retaining a single trajectory-level advantage across turns. Such shared credit can reinforce redundant replies after earlier successes and discourage useful follow-ups after earlier failures. We propose TurnWise, a turn-level GRPO framework that aligns learning with individual streaming decisions. TurnWise addresses the mismatch between trajectory-level feedback and turn-level interaction, promoting informative responses and reducing redundancy. Specifically, turn-level credit assignment uses silence-completed prefix scoring to construct group-relative advantages from each decision's remaining return, separating its learning signal from earlier rewards. Turn-mean policy optimization averages token losses within each turn and then across turns, giving response and waiting decisions equal weight regardless of output length. These complementary designs improve streaming policy learning without a learned critic or additional rollouts. Extensive experiments show our method outperforms prior RL approaches for streaming video understanding.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Scene Graph Anticipation (SGA) predicts object relationships in future video frames. Continuous-time SGA models evolve each subject-object pair with an independent Neural ODE, so relationships sharing an entity cannot influence one another during integration, and nothing prevents mutually exclusive predictions, such as "sitting on" and "standing on", for the same pair. We introduce CoupledSGA, which links such relationships through a Relationship Interaction Graph with graph attention inside the ODE vector field, and adds a Structural Consistency Loss ($\mathcal{L}_{SC}$) based on a matrix of mutually exclusive predicates.
  Developing CoupledSGA revealed a limitation of the evaluation protocol. With-Constraint evaluation keeps one predicate per group for each subject-object pair, so its R@K and mR@K cannot capture the within-group violations targeted by $\mathcal{L}_{SC}$: we prove they are unchanged whenever each group's winning predicate and its score are held fixed. We further show that the gradient of $\mathcal{L}_{SC}$ depends on predicate scores rather than ground-truth labels: for two-predicate rules it amplifies the ordering the model already holds, and when all scores in a group are equal it suppresses predicates in more exclusion rules more strongly, explaining why the penalty can lower recall while improving consistency.
  On Action Genome, activating $\mathcal{L}_{SC}$ after two warm-up epochs reduces R@20 from 42.21% to 35.18% while lowering the No-Constraint contradiction rate from 45.92% to 4.07% (matched-budget R@20: 40.48% CoupledSGA vs. 44.78% SceneSayerODE). CoupledSGA is a testbed, not a state-of-the-art claim: With-Constraint recall captures the cost of enforcing structural consistency but not the improvement. We recommend reporting No-Constraint results alongside a contradiction rate for methods that constrain predicate co-activation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Strong single-turn editing performance does not guarantee reliable multi-turn interaction: as edited outputs are repeatedly reused, small unintended changes can accumulate into severe visual drift. This long-term drift is often mitigated through training on multi-turn data or cross-turn rollouts, potentially altering pretrained priors, or through specialized inference-time mechanisms that are difficult to scale to foundation models. We introduce TailSteer, a final-stage training framework that improves multi-turn robustness while retaining the generative and editing capabilities acquired through earlier training. TailSteer follows a simple principle: keep early denoising dynamics close to those of the base model and concentrate preservation corrections near the trajectory tail, where fine-grained deviations can be suppressed. These corrections are learned from the editor's own on-policy denoising rollouts, using unchanged source content as a preservation reference without requiring curated ground-truth editing pairs. Experiments across the FLUX, SenseNova, and Qwen families show that TailSteer substantially reduces cumulative drift over 15-20 editing turns while largely preserving base-model performance on extensive text-to-image and single-image editing benchmarks. These results demonstrate that long-horizon editing robustness can be unlocked in foundation models after TailSteer, rather than by relearning their existing capabilities.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Generative video models are increasingly used as visual world simulators, yet their primary strength lies in rendering plausible pixels rather than reasoning about how a world should evolve. Coherent world evolution requires more than visual fidelity: when an event occurs, a model must infer its consequences for the entities in the world and then render the resulting state. For example, after a wild beast appears, the model should not only depict the wild beast, but also infer whether a nearby person should flee, take cover, or fight back based on the realized scene. Existing diffusion-based video models lack an explicit mechanism for maintaining world state and reasoning over such event-conditioned consequences, often producing visually plausible but behaviorally static or causally inconsistent outputs. To make this reasoning gap concrete and measurable, we propose a new problem, Out-of-Sight Causal Evolution, which requires a model to render an event, reason about its consequences for the evolving world state, and reveal a causally plausible outcome as the world continues to evolve, even out of sight. As a baseline reference solution, we introduce EvoWorld, a brain-renderer collaboration framework in which a vision-language model serves as the world brain: it observes generated segments, maintains an evolving account of the world, and reasons about plausible state transitions, while a segment-autoregressive video renderer realizes them visually. We further introduce EvoWorld-Data and EvoWorld-Bench for training and evaluating event realization, causal reactions, and revisit consistency. Experiments show that EvoWorld substantially improves event-conditioned causal evolution over recent models while preserving visual quality and consistency, establishing a reference point for reasoning-guided, autonomous world evolution beyond the current field of view.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Scientific image-analysis workflows require agents to select among diverse capabilities like description, detection, counting, and segmentation. Current multi-tool architectures conflate intent interpretation with visual execution, masking whether a failure arises from flawed reasoning (misrouting) or failed perception (invalid tool execution). This lack of auditability renders agents unreliable for high-stakes scientific automation. We introduce ZETA, an extensible architecture that operationalizes the decoupling of routing policy from action execution. ZETA resolves scientific intent before invoking specialized visual tools, treating measurement as a deterministic operation grounded in explicit geometry rather than generative output. This architecture exposes the "black box" of agent decision-making, allowing routing correctness and downstream execution to be evaluated as independent failure modes.
  To our knowledge, we provide the first controlled study in scientific vision-agent routing that isolates explicit routing-policy specification while holding the legal action interface fixed. Evaluation on a zero-shot benchmark of 51 scientific-intent queries shows that explicit routing semantics can substantially improve routing accuracy, particularly at semantically adjacent action boundaries, although improvements vary across models. For Qwen 3.8, explicit routing-policy specification improves segmentation routing accuracy from 6.2% to 75.0% and detection routing accuracy from 60.0% to 100%. ZETA's execution traces make routing decisions and downstream artifacts separately observable, providing a basis for distinguishing intent-selection errors from visual execution failures. ZETA establishes the necessary infrastructure to isolate execution errors from interpretive ones, a critical requirement for deploying reliable scientific vision agents.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Fine-grained visual reasoning is essential for remote sensing in urban management, yet remains underexplored in the evaluation of multimodal large language models (MLLMs). We introduce UMBench, a fine-grained urban remote sensing benchmark built from unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV) images for multiple urban management tasks such as traffic monitoring, urban construction, environmental quality, and disaster warning. Evaluations on UMBench reveal that current MLLMs often rely on scene-level priors and overlook fine-grained visual evidence. To address this issue, we further propose a novel evidence-first chain-of-thought method that directs models to inspect fine-grained visual evidence in a training-free manner. Experiments show that our method can improve the performance of both open-source and commercial MLLMs on UMBench.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Sign language generation (SLG) maps written sentences into signing motion while preserving both lexical content and fine-grained articulation. This requires a model to decide what signs should be expressed and to realize them through precise handshape, trajectory, facial expression and body coordination. Existing pose-based systems and reconstruction-only motion tokenizers often entangle these two decisions, leaving the generator without an explicit text-aligned intermediate target for what to sign before refining how to sign it. To address this issue, we introduce Semantic Residual Vector Quantization (SRVQ), a framework for SLG that frames generation as a two-level process moving from semantics to kinematics. The newly introduced SRV Quantizer learns a hierarchy in which the first residual level encodes sign content and subsequent levels refine pose, handshape, trajectory, and body coordination, all within a single quantizer and without separate semantic representations or external planning stages. Through word-aware conditioning for hierarchical code generation, a masked semantic generator predicts semantic codes and a kinematic generator completes the residual codes for full SMPL-X motion synthesis. We evaluate using retrieval and FID in a shared text-motion embedding space, alongside standard geometric metrics. On CSL-Daily and Phoenix-2014T, SRVQ achieves R@1 of 82.3 and 70.3, outperforming the previous SOTA by 30 and 46 points, respectively.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Few-step distillation offers efficient generation with diffusion and flow models, but preserving quality with only a few sampling steps remains challenging. Distribution-matching methods via score distillation provide a strong foundation, yet they rely on accurately tracking the student's changing distribution. At intermediate generation steps in few-step sampling, student-score estimation can be difficult, and commonly used estimators may describe a different distribution from the one used to update the student. Flow matching allows a more flexible approach to supervision through velocity fields constructed from the student's sampling process. Building on this perspective, we introduce Velocity Adversarial Distillation (VAD), which uses a learned critic to compare student and teacher fields and turn their disagreement into feedback for learning. VAD unifies score distillation, Fisher-based matching, and moment matching, also explaining existing heuristic MMD updates and delivering new training objectives. We further develop VA-TDM, which follows trajectory distribution matching: providing supervision for partial denoising along the student trajectory. Beyond this, VA-TDM combines transition-aware online velocity learning and dual-form VAD supervision across intermediate student transitions. Extensive results show that VA-TDM is powerful for learning few-step generators, achieving state-of-the-art four-step performance in both text-to-image and text-to-video generation across various backbones and scaling effectively to 33B MiniMax-H3. In particular, our VA-TDM-H3 notably outperforms the few-step Fast-H3 and is closer to the original many-step H3 according to human preference.

Abstract (英文): Egocentric human video offers a scalable source of manipulation priors for robot learning, yet translating raw data scale into generalizable robot policies demands rigorous data curation and training recipes. We introduce Open-HumanScale, the first fully open recipe for scaling egocentric pretraining to 50,000 hours, anchored by Open-HumanScale-50K---a curated egocentric video corpus densely annotated with hand motion and natural language. Leveraging this corpus, we systematically investigate pure human pretraining via closed-loop simulation evaluations and uncover an empirical log-linear scaling law linking pretraining data hours to downstream robot success rates. Grounded in these findings, we develop HumanWAM-Vanilla, a world--action model pretrained exclusively on 50k hours of egocentric human video. Upon robot post-training, HumanWAM-Vanilla reaches 92.08% success on RoboTwin 2.0 Clean2Clean and 68.88% on Clean2Random, alongside a 14.17% mean success rate and a 19.00 mean progress score on RoboDojo. To push the capability frontier further, we explore joint human--robot pretraining with HumanWAM-Goal, a visual-goal-conditioned world--action model. Hybrid data scaling unlocks broad zero-shot generalization across both simulation and physical benchmarks, propelling post-trained RoboTwin 2.0 performance to new state-of-the-art levels: 92.70% on Clean2Clean and a substantial leap to 84.60% on Clean2Random. On physical robots, HumanWAM-Goal further exhibits emergent embodied in-context learning, dynamically adapting its manipulation behavior to unseen visual goals without parameter updates. To support reproducible scaling research in embodied AI, we open-source the Open-HumanScale-50K corpus, data curation and pretraining pipelines, alongside the pretrained and post-trained HumanWAM checkpoints, inference code, and simulation evaluation suites.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Autoregressive image generation has recently emerged as a competitive visual generation paradigm, yet its generation order is often predefined or heuristic, such as next-patch prediction, random-order prediction, or one-step generation for specific scales. We observe that these strategies can cause models to overlook important structural dependencies in images, thereby limiting both structural robustness and reasoning capability. In this work, we propose a context-aware autoregressive generation framework, where the model dynamically determines the next token location based on global context and current local information. Rather than following a fixed or random order, our method allows the generation process to adapt to image structure and semantic dependencies. Extensive experiments show that context-aware generation significantly improves structural robustness across different image AR paradigms, including masked autoregressive and scale-wise autoregressive models. Beyond quantitative improvements, we further observe emerging reasoning properties: the model can better understand complex semantic instructions and generate images that more faithfully follow the given guidance. These findings suggest that generation order is a critical yet underexplored factor in image autoregressive modeling, and that context-aware decoding can unlock stronger reasoning-aware visual generation capabilities. Code is available at https://github.com/Hpesojyjx/Saccades-AR.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Ask SD3.5-Medium for twelve of an object and then, from the same starting noise, for twenty-five. The picture keeps its layout, but the count changes about as much as it would under a new random seed, and in no consistent direction. We study what number words do once a request passes about ten objects, in five open text-to-image models. Holding the object and the starting noise fixed, we change one thing at a time, and people who never see the request count 3,088 of the images. Every model follows the number for small crowds, but only up to a point, its reach: SD3.5-M and FLUX.1-dev stop following it soon after ten, while FLUX.2 [klein] and Qwen-Image follow it loosely up to twenty-five. Beyond SD3.5-M's reach, the coarse part of the starting noise fixes the composition of the picture but not how many objects it holds: redrawing only the noise finer than an eighth of the image keeps the layout as well as a new number does, and already moves the count two-thirds as far. The number word still reaches the denoiser and acts in its first steps, but nothing we tried gives it a clear direction. Shown instead as a noised grid in the starting latent, the count is drawn: people find it in 59% of images, against 4% when it is only told. Past ten, an exact-match score barely separates a model that follows the number loosely from one that ignores it, and a relative-error score can even prefer a constant guess of sixteen. A same-noise test with a few requests per object tells them apart.

</details>

Abstract (英文): A central yet under-explored question in Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) is where to connect---how visual information from a hierarchical vision encoder should be wired into the layer-wise semantics of a large language model. Mainstream MLLMs inject a fixed single-layer visual representation into the LLM and treat all LLM layers as a single consumer, ignoring that different layers demand different visual granularities. Recent multi-layer fusion methods either compress hierarchical features on the encoder side or rely on predefined sparse or hierarchical layer-to-layer connections, and therefore stop short of modeling adaptive cross-layer interactions between the ViT and LLM hierarchies. We address this gap with DGP (Dynamic Gated Pathways), a routing mechanism that establishes all-to-all connections between every ViT layer and every LLM layer, with each LLM layer dynamically gating its preference over visual layers conditioned on its own semantic state. Built on top of LLaVA-1.5-7B and 13B, DGP delivers consistent gains over the LLaVA-1.5-7B baseline (e.g., +4.2 on SQA$^{I}$, +3.3 on MM-Vet, +3.1 on VizWiz and LLaVA$^{W}$) and surpasses prior multi-layer visual fusion methods on most benchmarks. Layer-wise routing analyses further show that different LLM layers do select different visual granularities on demand, supporting both the effectiveness and interpretability of the proposed dynamic gated pathways. The code is available: https://github.com/YingyingYan1/DGP.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Automated identification of individual animals is moving from small curated studies toward shared benchmarks and reusable foundation models. This progress is useful, but current evaluation leaves the evidence behind a match unresolved, i.e., high retrieval accuracy does not show whether a model used animal features or identity-correlated shortcuts. We address this with Counterfactual Foreground Retrieval (CFR), an evidence-aware evaluation protocol. CFR evaluates the same query-gallery task under different views: full image, foreground-only, and shortcut-only. The full-image measures standard retrieval performance; the foreground-only measures foreground-supported retrieval; and shortcut-only view measure how much identity signal is recoverable from background, or silhouette. This turns a retrieval score into an evidence profile: how well the model performs on the original images, how much of that performance remains when only the animal is visible, and how much identity signal remains recoverable when the animal is removed. We release around 0.5M masks for 34 datasets and code for mask generation and CFR evaluation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): State-of-the-art feedforward reconstruction models perform poorly on in-the-wild object-centric photo collections. They are trained largely on single-session object captures whose images vary in viewpoint while the object and its environment remain nearly fixed. Public photo archives contain a different form of multi-view data. They hold found photos of the same object or design: photos taken independently by many people across years, environments, cameras, and sometimes physical units. In this work, we introduce Trainspotting, a dataset of 12,068 objects and 311,886 photos from Wikimedia Commons. We reconstruct each object to recover camera poses, multi-view stereo depth, and object masks. We categorize the objects into three regimes: objects fixed at one site, objects that move between sites, and designs shared by many physical units. Evaluating recent feedforward reconstruction models on Trainspotting shows that they fail most severely in the last two regimes. Fine-tuning VGGT-$Ω$ on Trainspotting significantly improves camera pose, depth, and 3D geometry on held-out objects. We observe similar improvements on the independent NAVI in-the-wild benchmark, which contains photos of each object in different environments. Performance on conventional benchmarks remains largely unchanged.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Knowing that a pedestrian is occluded is useful to a robot even when the detector cannot recover the hidden pixels, but occlusion estimators are normally trained on occlusion annotations that deployment settings rarely have. We show that occlusion can be read off two distance estimates a camera-only pipeline already computes. Apparent height is largely unchanged by occlusion, because the detected box still spans the whole person; monocular metric depth is pulled toward the occluder, because the nearest surface inside the box is what a depth map reports. Only their ordering matters, so no focal length, stature, or depth scale is needed, provided the depth model's error is close to a pure gain, a condition that can be checked without labels. The score detects heavy occlusion on KITTI with AUC 0.909 (0.888 over all annotated pedestrians, including those the detector misses), requires no training and no occlusion labels, and is not a proxy for range: within fixed distance bands it holds AUC 0.894-0.925 while true distance alone sits near chance. On synthetic CARLA scenes with measured visibility it reaches 0.819, against 0.644 for distance alone. We arrived here after measuring three standard architectural responses to occlusion, depth fusion, occlusion-simulating augmentation, and attention, none of which improved detection outside its training distribution in the configurations we tested, and after finding that a detector trained on German roads loses half its accuracy on self-annotated street scenes in Lagos, Nigeria. Signals that need no local annotation are worth more in settings that benchmarks do not represent.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Computer-use agents (CUAs) have recently achieved superhuman performance on several long-horizon benchmarks. However, their computational cost limits their use in long-horizon tasks and real-time domains such as video games. A key bottleneck is representing each screenshot as thousands of visual tokens, which accumulate into long contexts as agents reason over extended interaction histories. We observe that most of these visual tokens are either semantically irrelevant to the agent's next action or temporally redundant across screenshots. Building on this observation, we introduce Visual Token Routing (VTR), a joint training framework for learning sparse visual representations that preserve the information needed for action prediction. In VTR, we train the vision-language model together with a lightweight spatio-temporal router that examines each image region in the current and previous screenshots and decides whether its visual token enters the language model. We optimize the router using an adaptive loss to keep the number of visual tokens as small as possible while minimizing performance degradation. With just 2,000 training steps, VTR matches the performance of the full-resolution model on OSWorld, CUA-World, and video game action prediction, while using 2.5-5.8x fewer visual tokens. These savings reduce inference cost by up to 3.3x and speed up prefill by up to 2.9x. VTR also outperforms lowering the image resolution and other baselines for dropping tokens, by up to 12.8 points on OSWorld. Overall, VTR enables computer-use agents to process only the visual tokens they need, allowing them to be used in long-horizon tasks and real-time domains at a fraction of the computational cost. Code and models are available at https://pranjal2041.github.io/vtr

</details>

Abstract (英文): We introduce LongBanana, to our knowledge the first expert-designed and expert-verified benchmark for long-context multi-reference image synthesis in the age of agent-driven image generation. The task requires retrieving dispersed visual evidence, rejecting distractors, binding entities and relations, and composing one coherent final image from multiple references. LongBanana contains 90 carefully designed tasks organized by a 3-by-3 taxonomy; each task includes about 32 reference images, 20 constraints, and 50 evaluation items, yielding 4,670 expert-specified atomic evaluation items in total. Its graph-grounded evaluation design instantiates each task with reference images and an entity-relation graph, then converts node, edge, and whole-graph obligations into checklist items for Content Fidelity, Relational Binding, and Global Constraint Satisfaction. Reviewed expert labels include a 15% double-labeled subset with 89.4% exact agreement and 0.83 quadratic weighted kappa. We further introduce LongBanana-Gen, an agent-harness framework for long-horizon image generation; experiments show that minimal agent harnesses improve over direct generation, while a ReAct-Plan-Exec-Reflect (RPR) workflow yields harness-dependent gains by strengthening evidence grounding and repair. Together, LongBanana provides an auditable testbed for measuring dense visual evidence use and a shared target for developing stronger agent-driven image-generation systems. Code and data are available at the project page: https://long-banana.com/.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Existing continuous space-time video super-resolution methods often infer motion from only two endpoint frames and model the two motion directions independently, making them vulnerable to ambiguous correspondence and inconsistent trajectories. We introduce a continuous STVSR framework that combines long-context encoding, continuous HR feature lifting, and an endpoint-anchored PixelSpline. Long-range observations provide more reliable endpoint representations, while continuous upsampling constructs detailed appearance features directly on the requested HR grid. PixelSpline explicitly models each pixel's motion as a single continuous trajectory shared by both endpoints. Using this trajectory, our method warps endpoint colors into a coarse intermediate frame and aligns the HR features for residual reconstruction. Experiments across spatial and temporal upsampling settings demonstrate that the proposed framework offers a favorable quality--efficiency trade-off.

</details>

Abstract (英文): For many computer vision tasks, neural networks can be trained to produce a low average error, yet they still fail on seemingly simple scenes. Reducing these failures requires first finding them, but they may not be in a fixed dataset. We study procedural adversarial learning (PAL), a framework of minimax training and adversarial evaluation which searches the input space of a procedural generator for difficult instances. The procedural generator is an interpretable program mapping a low-dimensional vector of control parameters (e.g. layout, lighting, pose) to a rendered image and its label. We introduce the Simple-PAT algorithm, which uses zeroth-order optimization in the procedural generator's parameter space to propose adversarial training samples that are generated on demand. We also create PAL-Dojo, a collection of efficient procedural generators for surface normal estimation, image segmentation, and human pose estimation. Against a cross-model black-box adversary with 50M aggregate queries, Simple-PAT reduces worst-case error by a geometric mean of 3.15x compared to standard training and 2.63x compared to the strongest robust training baseline. Code is available at https://github.com/princeton-vl/procedural-adversarial-learning.

</details>

Abstract (英文): JEPA-style world models learn action-conditioned latent dynamics without reconstructing observations, but distributional regularization alone does not ensure that their representations preserve information needed for control. We identify static collapse, a failure mode in which the encoder preserves episode-specific static content while suppressing manipulation-relevant state changes. Because static content is temporally predictable yet diverse across episodes, this failure can persist despite regularizers such as SIGReg. A controlled study on OGBench-Cube shows that introducing randomized backgrounds substantially degrades planning performance while leaving the underlying manipulation task unchanged. To address this problem, we propose motion-guided contrastive regularization. Our approach compares predicted latent transitions with transitions to candidate observations from the same episode, reducing the usefulness of static background identity for distinguishing action outcomes. Optical-flow similarities provide soft contrastive targets, allowing candidates with similar motion to receive similar supervision. Combined with latent prediction and SIGReg, our objective encourages representations to preserve within-episode state distinctions and similarities between related transitions. Our method improves planning success over LeWorldModel (LeWM) from 52% to 78% on OGBench-Cube with randomized backgrounds and from 14% to 54% on LIBERO-Object, while maintaining comparable performance on four other visual-control benchmarks.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Vision-language-action models are typically built on a simple premise: perception is first compressed by visual encoders, and action reasoning begins only afterwards. Yet manipulation often hinges on precisely the visual signals most vulnerable to this abstraction, e.g., local geometry, boundaries, depth, and subtle state changes. This raises a fundamental question: should action policies inherit a fixed visual endpoint, or directly access evolving visual computation? We introduce NEO-VLA, a structurally encoder-free vision-language-action framework. Images are tokenized by lightweight patch convolutional layers and evolve jointly with language throughout the backbone, allowing a flow-matching action expert to draw from instruction-conditioned visual representations at different depths. This simple change reveals a consistent pattern. Native architectures outperform encoder-based counterparts under controlled comparisons; full fine-tuning reduces but does not erase the gap, and additional scale alone cannot recover it. More strikingly, layer-wise probing shows that the action-critical signals often peak well before the final visual representation. Across diverse manipulation settings and distribution shifts, NEO-VLA consequently achieves stronger and more robust control. Together, these results suggest that embodied control benefits from access to visual representations across the computational hierarchy, rather than a fixed encoder output.

</details>

Abstract (英文): High-definition (HD) vector maps are essential to trajectory forecasting, yet existing methods often use them only as passive context and remain dependent on identity-consistent tracking histories. We propose a unified geometry-centric forecasting framework that treats road-space geometry as an active computational substrate for both future prediction and history recovery under tracking-available and no-tracking conditions. The framework uses a common road-space representation and topology-guided decoupled decoder architecture across both settings, while differing only in how motion history is constructed. With reliable tracking, agent history is incrementally aggregated across shifting local frames for efficient streaming inference. Without tracking, frame-wise detections are projected into lane-space occupancy memories, from which target-specific history is recovered through geometry-conditioned querying. The decoder then generates topology-consistent motion intents and realizes them as final trajectories using surrounding-agent interactions. Experiments on Argoverse 2 (AV2), Argoverse 1 (AV1), and the Waymo Open Motion Dataset (WOMD) show that the proposed framework achieves competitive performance under reliable tracking and supports low-latency streaming inference, reaching 44.32 ms per frame with pre-encoded maps. Under no-tracking conditions, the occupancy-based pathway preserves most of the tracking-available performance on the primary K = 6 metrics, with relative increases in minFDE 6 , minADE 6 , and MR 6 limited to 6%–8% on AV2 and below 3% on AV1 and WOMD. Additional validation on real sensor data with annotated detections and a tracking-by-detection association pipeline shows that the occupancy-based pathway remains robust to tracking identity errors, whereas identity-dependent methods degrade substantially. These results demonstrate that actively exploiting road-space geometry can substantially reduce trajectory forecasting’s reliance on upstream tracking.

</details>

Abstract (英文): With the rapid shrinkage of Arctic sea ice, predicting its spatio-temporal changes has become an important technical basis for studies of polar climate. Existing physics-driven and data-driven paradigms show respective advantages in representing sea-ice process mechanisms and learning complex spatio-temporal patterns; however, both remain challenged in balancing prediction accuracy with physics consistency. Therefore, this study proposes a daily-scale spatio-temporal sea ice thickness (SIT) prediction method driven by both data and physics, namely the Physics-Constrained Generative Adversarial Network (PCGAN). To incorporate sea-ice physics principles into the data-driven prediction process, PCGAN is designed at three levels: the generator, the discriminators, and physics-rule constraints. First, a Physics-Augmented Cross-Process Interaction (PA-CPI) module is designed in the generator to enhance the ability of the model to represent the physically coupled mechanisms among multiple geophysics variables, including sea ice, snow, wind fields, and temperature. Second, a spatio-temporal dual-discriminator architecture is proposed to jointly constrain the local spatial structures and temporal increments of SIT. Finally, the freezing-degree-day growth relationship and the daily-scale thickness change-rate relationship are transformed into bounded physics consistency scores and embedded into temporal adversarial learning to improve the physics plausibility of the generator-predicted results. Experimental results in the Arctic Ocean during the cold season show that PCGAN maintains stable performance under different random initializations. Its average RMSE is 0.1035 m, which is 12.73% lower than that of MSS-STFormer, the best-performing baseline model. Physics-related statistical metrics, such as the anomalous thinning rate, also show that the predictions of PCGAN are closer to the observed statistical characteristics than those of the other deep learning models. Multidimensional spatio-temporal analyses further confirm the stable predictive performance of PCGAN under different prediction conditions. The interpretability analysis shows that the model’s responses to multiple geophysical factors are broadly consistent with established physical understanding of sea-ice evolution. Ablation experiments and threshold-sensitivity analyses verify the effectiveness of the key model structures and the physics consistency constraints. PCGAN demonstrates the potential of incorporating physics consistency constraints into data-driven frameworks to improve SIT prediction accuracy and physical plausibility, providing methodological support for Arctic SIT prediction during the cold season.

</details>

Abstract (英文): To meet the growing need for timely, frequent, and consistent long-term land surface information, the Annual NLCD Collection 1.0 integrates methodologies from the National Land Cover Database (NLCD) and the Land Change Monitoring, Assessment, and Projection (LCMAP) initiative with modern geospatial deep-learning approaches. The resulting product suite provides six annual 30-m products for the conterminous United States (CONUS) spanning 1985–2023: Land Cover, Land Cover Change, Land Cover Confidence, Fractional Impervious Surface, Impervious Descriptor, and Spectral Change Day of Year. The overall accuracy of the NLCD 16-class thematic land cover product was 64.7 ± 0.5% across the study period and increased to 76.9 ± 0.4% when agreement included a match with either the primary or alternate reference label. Overall accuracy remained stable over time ranging from 64.5% to 65.0%. Even with increased temporal resolution, Annual NLCD maintained comparable accuracy to that of previous NLCD products mapped at 2–3-year intervals. Change detection accuracy for Annual NLCD was low, consistent with the difficulty of detecting and dating annual land cover transitions. User's and producer's accuracies varied considerably across change themes. Water gain and loss, forest loss, and shrubland gain were the better-detected change types, while most other change themes had accuracy <40% when allowing a ± 2-year buffer to define agreement. Despite its finer thematic classification scheme, the Annual NLCD product substantially improved change accuracy relative to LCMAP. Based on the Annual NLCD maps, approximately 19.4% of CONUS experienced at least one land cover change during 1985–2023. Annual NLCD products provide a consistent annual record of land cover and land use information to support diverse downstream applications.

</details>

Abstract (英文): A thorough understanding of the global carbon pools and cycle is essential for understanding and predicting the effects of climate change. Coastal waters play a key role in the global carbon cycle but remain poorly understood due to their optical complexity, high spatial variability, and sensitivity to climate change. Satellite remote sensing data can provide high spatial and temporal resolution for carbon monitoring at local, regional, and global scales. This study estimates the potential of Sentinel-2 MSI (S2) and Sentinel-3 OLCI (S3) for mapping carbon fractions in coastal waters. In situ hyperspectral reflectance measurements and concentrations of different carbon fractions, namely Total Organic Carbon (TOC), Dissolved Organic Carbon (DOC), Particulate Organic Carbon (POC), and Dissolved Inorganic Carbon (DIC), were used. Bio-optical and physical water parameters, as well as carbon fractions, were measured in the coastal areas of the eastern Baltic Sea from 2023 to 2024. In situ reflectance spectra were recalculated to S2 and S3 band reflectance using their respective spectral response functions. Regression models, including linear regression and machine learning methods, were developed and validated against in situ data to estimate carbon fractions. For S2, the combinations of green and red or red-edge bands achieved the best performance in carbon retrieval, with R 2 values of 0.67, 0.64, and 0.56 for TOC, DOC, and DIC, respectively. The corresponding MAPE values ranged from 14% to 18%, and biases were close to zero. Slightly higher accuracies were obtained with S3 simulations. The S3 models that used reflectance at 490, 620, and 665 nm produced the most accurate estimates for both TOC ( R 2 = 0.71) and DOC ( R 2 = 0.67). For DIC, the best predictive model combined information from 510, 665, and 709 nm bands, achieving R 2 of 0.62. Among the tested optical proxies, CDOM absorption at 443 nm showed the most consistent relationship with TOC, DOC, and DIC ( R 2 = 0.56–0.30). The study represents a significant step forward in the remote sensing of coastal waters, and if adopted, the proposed carbon-fraction products could enable substantial progress across various fields, from carbon-cycle research to monitoring and policymaking.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Agroforestry is a land-use practice that integrates trees into agricultural lands, supporting food security and rural livelihoods while delivering both climate adaptation and mitigation benefits. However, current large-scale assessments often rely on coarse tree canopy cover or biomass products to approximate agroforestry, which results in biases estimates of the production and ecological effects from agroforestry. Here, we produced a 100 m resolution map of the spatial extent of agroforestry lands in Africa for 2019, using 10 m resolution tree canopy cover data derived from PlanetScope and Sentinel-2 imagery. This map explicitly delineates croplands where agroforestry system is practised by distinguishing tree spatial patterns within croplands, focusing on irregularly scattered trees and trees aligned in rows. Scattered trees are identified using tree-pixel distance metrics, whereas trees planted in rows are detected using a U-Net model trained with ~38,000 manually labelled tree-row features. The identified agroforestry lands with scattered trees and trees in rows on cropland were validated against ground-based sites collected from the literature and Google Earth imagery, yielding an overall accuracy of 77.2%. This agroforestry dataset with explicit spatial tree arrangements can served as a baseline for assessing environmental impacts and crop yield change of agroforestry ecosystems, as well as for supporting the development of process-based agroforestry models.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate geolocation in GNSS-denied mountainous environments remains a significant challenge for navigation and field operations. While vision-based methods using terrain features like skylines offer a promising passive solution, their performance is often hindered by unknown camera orientation, low feature discriminability across vast areas, and severe degradation under nighttime conditions. To address these limitations, this paper presents a novel visual geolocation framework that synergistically couples ephemeris-based orientation correction with multi-scale skyline feature matching. First, an Edge-Enhanced SegFormer-B3 (EESFormer-B3) network is developed for robust skyline extraction from query images, where a lightweight Edge Gate mechanism is integrated to refine boundary precision, particularly under low-light scenarios. Second, we propose a camera heading estimation method utilizing solar and lunar ephemeris, eliminating reliance on external heading sensors and drastically reducing the matching search space. Third, for matching, a hierarchical composite feature is designed, encoding the skyline's global shape trends, local directional variations, and dominant structural distributions to ensure robustness against repetitive terrain patterns. Extensive experiments were conducted in a complex 700 km² mountainous region. The results demonstrate that our method achieves a localization success rate of 82% (daytime) and 76% (nighttime) within a 250-meter error threshold. The ephemeris-based correction alone accelerates the matching process by over 30 times compared to an unconstrained orientation search. This work provides a reliable, efficient, and illumination-invariant geolocation solution, with strong potential for applications in autonomous navigation, search and rescue, and other field missions in GNSS-compromised natural environments.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Quantifying greenhouse gas (GHG) emissions from livestock operations is a prerequisite for reconciling bottom-up inventories with atmospheric observations. However, global GHG reporting remains limited by a lack of spatially explicit data on animal waste management systems (AWMS), forcing reliance on coarse regional assumptions rather than operation-level observations. Existing geospatial foundation models partly address this spatial gap by mapping cattle-operation footprints that provide upstream spatial context, but contextual detection alone cannot identify the manure-management features within them. To address this gap, we introduce the Bovine Slurry Index (BSI), an empirical Sentinel-2 spectral index for detecting liquid/slurry manure ponds within known cattle-operation footprints. The primary formulation, BSI-E, uses calibrated narrow-NIR (B8A) and SWIR-1 (B11) reflectance bounds derived from manure pond samples in the United States and China. The index targets the empirically observed intermediate NIR-SWIR response of manure ponds relative to clear water, vegetation, dry holding pens, and exposed soil. Calibration used 2,730 pond samples from the U.S. and China, and segmentation was evaluated against 945 reference polygons across all five geographic regions. BSI-E produced stronger pond-to-background contrast and higher segmentation accuracy than evaluated water indices, with dataset-level intersection-over-union (IoU) ranging from 0.26 in Brazil to 0.68 in the United States and precision reaching 0.91. Optimal thresholds varied geographically from 0.25 to 1.00, and performance was strongest in dryland feedlot systems and lower in humid or spectrally mixed settings. BSI provides a target-specific spectral method for generating operation-scale evidence that could inform AWMS activity data within these footprints.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The low spatial resolution (300–400 km) of Gravity Recovery and Climate Experience (GRACE) and the 11-month data gap between GRACE and GRACE Follow-On (GRACE-FO) complicate the evaluation of long-term ice mass loss at sub-region scale. Although several studies have downscaled GRACE data for water storage evaluation, efforts to downscale ice mass change are limited. Here, we utilized multiscale geographically-weighted regression (MGWR) to improve the spatial resolution of GRACE-derived ice mass change from 0.25° to 5 km in Greenland Ice Sheet (GrIS). The reconstructed results after downscaling demonstrate consistent temporal variation and reduced noise compared to other reconstruction methods for sub-regions and the entire GrIS. They can capture finer signals of ice mass change while maintaining original spatial characteristics. Validation against free air gravity anomalies changes also demonstrates the superior performance of the downscaled results over the reconstructed CSR Mascon data. For overlapping lines, the mean correlation coefficient (CC) increases from 0.13 to 0.32, with the mean root mean square error (RMSE) decreasing from 1.36 to 1.13. At the intersection points, the mean CC improves from 0.06 to 0.30, and the mean RMSE is reduced from 1.33 to 1.05. They are further used to detect the contributions of Greenland Blocking (GB) and North Atlantic Oscillation (NAO) to high-resolution ice mass change through precipitation and runoff in four sub-periods, during which the driving factors affecting ice mass change differ significantly. Additionally, NAO and GB can influence ice mass change through solid ice discharge by impacting surface temperature and sea-ice concentration.

</details>

Abstract (英文): This study evaluates the accuracy and usability of two low-cost unmanned aerial system (UAS) multispectral sensors—MicaSense RedEdge and SlantRange 3p—by comparing their reflectance measurements and NDVI to those of the NEON Imaging Spectrometer (NIS), a well-calibrated airborne hyperspectral sensor. Field validation was conducted using calibrated reflectance tarps, and spectrally resampled NIS data served as a reference standard. Reflectance comparisons showed both sensors performed well across most land cover types but exhibited systematic biases that prevented statistical equivalence in most bands. The MicaSense sensor generally overestimated reflectance, while the SlantRange sensor underestimated it, particularly in the Green and NIR bands. These biases propa-gated into NDVI calculations, where both UAS sensors reported lower NDVI values than the NIS, with SlantRange showing a larger negative bias. Despite the lower NDVI values and artifacts, including BRDF effects at image edges and background noise over dark sur-faces, the results support the viability of UAS-based multispectral sensing for ecological applications requiring moderate to high accuracy. The study also highlights the critical importance of radiometric calibration and sensor-specific limitations in reflectance range. This comparison framework provides a foundation for broader cross-platform validation efforts and helps inform best practices for ecological remote sensing using low-cost UAS systems.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Live fuel moisture content (LFMC) influences vegetation flammability, but field measurements are too sparse to describe its variation over large areas. We present, to our knowledge, the first LFMC framework that combines observations from multiple optical satellite sensors within the same trained models. More than two decades of GlobeLFMC 2.0 measurements are matched to Terra and Aqua Moderate Resolution Imaging Spectroradiometer (MODIS), Visible Infrared Imaging Radiometer Suite (VIIRS), Landsat 8/9, Sentinel-2 and Sentinel-3. We screen the vegetation surrounding sampling locations and adjust reflectance for differences between sensors before calculating vegetation indices. Weather, topography and time of year provide additional information. Separate models are trained for grasses, shrubs and trees. We test prediction on dates not used for training at sites where other dates contribute to training. At each site, records using the same satellite image are kept in either the training or the test set. The final Random Forest models achieve R2 values of 0.715, 0.693 and 0.691, respectively. Models trained on the combined archive give higher R2 and lower root mean square error than models trained on one satellite product in all 18 product–vegetation comparisons on identical test observations. Paired 95% confidence intervals support the improvement in 16 comparisons. Testing at sites excluded from training gives lower accuracy. The method produces LFMC maps at the selected satellite product’s resolution, from 20–30 m to 300–500 m.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Ground clutter persists as residual stationary or recurrent echo in gridded reflectivity-only weather radar products, then is converted into spurious light rainfall when reflectivity is mapped to rain rate. This study presents an image-level quality control filter for Météo-France MeteoNet composites from northwestern France. Trained annotators labeled 346,629 pixels from clear-air and weather windows; complete events were withheld from training during validation. Intensity, spatial, temporal, and object features were combined in a random forest (RF) that separated clutter from weather echo on unseen events, reaching F1 = 0.833 with weather echo preservation of 0.952 and clutter suppression of 0.763. Choosing a 95% preservation operating point from training events alone maintained 0.953 preservation on outer test events; a gradient-boosted decision tree model achieved comparable discrimination. Rain gauges supported period screening, station-level recurrence review, and paired 30-min quantitative precipitation estimation (QPE). At nine reviewed recurrent-echo stations, targeted RF gating reduced false alarm ratio from 0.277 to 0.211 and mean absolute error from 0.0549 to 0.0526 mm by filtering the light rain echoes that produced false precipitation, while retaining most genuine rainfall. The resulting framework filters false precipitation effectively at locations with recurrent clutter, providing an auditable event-grouped workflow for quality control of reflectivity-only radar imagery.

Abstract (英文): Sudden Stratospheric Warmings (SSWs) are rare polar stratospheric extremes far less documented over Antarctica than the Arctic. A pair of successive midwinter Antarctic SSWs occurred in July–August 2024, featuring severe polar vortex distortion and sharp stratospheric temperature rises. Using MERRA-2/ERA5 reanalysis and Aura MLS satellite ozone data, we diagnose wave 1 and wave 2 planetary wave forcing and associated ozone variations. Abnormally strong upward-propagating Southern Hemisphere tropospheric planetary waves drove the 2024 SSW. Wave 1 triggered the first July warming peak, while amplified wave 2 dominated the second intense vortex disruption in early August. These waves delivered westward momentum that weakened the polar night jet and destabilized the vortex. Accelerated Brewer–Dobson circulation (BDC) enhanced poleward ozone transport, raising polar ozone levels and supplying extra stratospheric heat. Wave–ozone coupling shows clear selectivity: wave 1 induces negative total ozone column anomalies across 15°S–50°S midlatitudes, whereas wave 2 generates broad positive ozone signals over 50°S–65°S and primarily controls polar ozone enrichment. Fading planetary wave activity after the warming peaks gradually restored the polar vortex and stratospheric thermal structure. This study provides key observational evidence for rare Antarctic SSWs and advances understanding of stratosphere–troposphere coupling and polar ozone dynamics under global warming.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Remote-sensing image change captioning (RSICC) generally assumes correctly paired bitemporal images and focuses on isolated sentence descriptions, limiting applicability to archives where the correct temporal counterpart may be unknown. We introduce the Noisy-Reference Paragraph-Level Change-Captioning (NRP-CC) task, which requires retrieving the post-change observation from a noisy candidate pool before generating a coherent multi-statement paragraph. We construct the SECOND-ParCap dataset with image-level semantic annotations and paragraph-level change descriptions, and propose a Weakly Supervised Detection-Guided Historical-Cascaded Network (WSDHC-Net). WSDHC-Net leverages Dual Similarity Matching (DS-Matching) for retrieval from noisy candidate pools and employs weakly supervised detection to mine semantic cues for the Historical-Cascaded Paragraph Refinement Change-Captioning (HCPR-CC) module, which generates multi-statement paragraphs describing scene changes. We further introduce SA-ROUGE to evaluate sentence-level correspondence across the generated statements. On SECOND-ParCap, WSDHC-Net achieves Acc@1 scores of 99.53%, 99.29%, and 98.52% under appearance-hard retrieval with 5, 10, and 20 candidates, respectively. In a separate captioning benchmark using identical frozen retrieved inputs across all compared methods, WSDHC-Net attains ROUGE-L and SA-ROUGE scores of 0.5017 and 0.5016, respectively, under reference-assisted sentence-level evaluation; the corresponding paragraph-level scores are 0.2749 and 1.9305. These results support the feasibility of integrating temporal-correspondence retrieval with paragraph-level change description in NRP-CC.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Ma bamboo (Dendrocalamus latiflorus Munro) is an ecologically and economically important sympodial bamboo species widely cultivated in southern China. Mapping its spatial distribution with satellite imagery remains challenging due to spectral response overlaps between Ma bamboo and other forest types. This study assessed the potential of integrating seasonal spectral variation and a targeted SWIR2–RE1 spectral contrast from Sentinel-2 to improve Ma bamboo mapping. Four normalized seasonal metrics were derived from winter and summer observations. A Ma Bamboo Spectral Index (MBSI) was developed based on the normalized contrast between SWIR2 and RE1. Four feature configurations were evaluated using XGBoost and nested spatial cross-validation. SHAP and accumulated local effects were further employed to interpret the model. The results showed that the model integrating annual spectral variables, seasonal metrics, and MBSI performed best, with an overall accuracy of 0.833, an F1 score of 0.819, and an AUC of 0.875. Seasonal metrics primarily improved producer’s accuracy, whereas MBSI contributed more to user’s accuracy, indicating complementary effects on reducing omission and commission errors. MBSI was the most important predictor, while RE1c was the most important seasonal metric and showed the strongest interaction with MBSI. These results show that combining seasonal spectral variation with the SWIR2–RE1 contrast can improve the completeness and reliability of Ma bamboo mapping and provide an interpretable framework for mapping in southern China.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Rapid post-event landslide mapping is essential for emergency response and disaster management; however, accurately delineating rainfall-induced landslides remains challenging because of their fragmented morphology, heterogeneous landscapes, and the complementary nature of Earth Observation data. Although recent deep learning approaches have demonstrated promising performance using optical imagery, the contribution of different geo-auxiliary information to landslide segmentation and its transferability across regions remain insufficiently understood. This study proposes a Residual Dual-Branch SegFormer that integrates heterogeneous geospatial information through a lightweight residual Convolutional Neural Network (CNN) encoder and a novel Multi-Scale Geo-Auxiliary Gated Fusion Module (MGFM), enabling adaptive feature fusion while preserving the original SegFormer architecture. Using a multimodal dataset constructed from the March 2023 Tropical Cyclone Freddy event in southern Malawi, we systematically evaluate RGB imagery combined with optical, terrain, and Sentinel-1 SAR-derived auxiliary layers. The framework is assessed on a geographically independent test area and further validated through zero-shot transfer on the public Landslide4Scene and Lushan datasets. Experimental results demonstrate the following: (1) Integrating pre- and post-event SAR coherence with RGB imagery achieves the best overall performance when applied to areas that match the training data, reaching a mean Intersection over Union (mIoU) of 80.56% and an F1-score of 78.03% in the independent Malawi test area. (2) RGB combined with slope represents the most effective among the evaluated static topographic configurations in zero-shot transfer to the two external datasets, consistently outperforming the RGB baseline. (3) The proposed framework exhibits strong cross-region generalization, achieving the highest transfer performance on both external datasets while demonstrating that SAR coherence provides the most discriminative auxiliary information for post-event rainfall-induced landslide segmentation. These findings provide practical guidance for designing robust multimodal deep learning frameworks for operational landslide mapping.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Complex rotational motion of a ship induces severe range migration and time-varying Doppler modulation in radar echoes. The time-domain generalized Radon–Fourier transform (TGRFT) can jointly compensate for these effects, but its multidimensional search over the initial range and all motion coefficients leads to a high computational burden. This paper proposes a two-dimensional frequency-domain GRFT (2D-FGRFT) for fast motion-parameter estimation and inverse synthetic aperture radar (ISAR) imaging of ships. The two-dimensional frequency-domain expression of a radar echo with a polynomial slant-range history is first derived, based on which a corresponding frequency-domain matched filter is constructed. The matched filter is parameterized only by the second- and higher-order frequency-domain motion coefficients. By exploiting the structure of the derived expression, a two-dimensional inverse fast Fourier transform (2D-IFFT) is performed after matched filtering, replacing the explicit searches over the initial range and first-order motion coefficient while preserving coherent integration of echoes from multiple scatterers on the ship. Finally, the motion parameters are determined from the matched-filter parameters and the peak locations obtained through constant false alarm rate (CFAR) detection, enabling fast ISAR imaging of the ship. Computer simulations and experimental results validate the computational efficiency, noise robustness, and overall performance of the proposed algorithm.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Mountain vegetation change is commonly summarized as a mean greening or browning trend, or as the displacement of a discrete boundary; both can miss changes in the shape of the elevation profile of vegetation greenness. Here we tested whether the greenness profile on the southern flank of the Tianshan Mountains reorganized between 1989 and 2024, using a Landsat transect along the Dushanzi–Kuqa (G217) highway. NDVI and EVI2 profiles were constructed over a fixed 1000–4100 m elevation domain using only sampling locations valid in all comparison periods; profiles were mean-centered, and the early-to-recent contrast was tested with spatial-block permutations across 36 combinations of temporal window, corridor width, elevation-bin width, and index. All 36 combinations showed significant shape change (p ≤ 0.005) with cross-scenario concordance, and the contrast persisted after adjustment for cross-sensor differences. The contrast followed a consistent three-band structure, with relative decreases at 1100–1950 m, increases at 2550–3300 m, and renewed decreases at 3550–4100 m. Absolute greenness increased almost everywhere (more than 97% of 25,334 samples), but the increase was four to five times larger in the mid-elevation band than in the lower and upper bands, varied eightfold along the transect, and was organized primarily by elevation and along-transect position (spatially cross-validated random-forest R2 ≈ 0.66). The vegetation contrast was not directionally consistent with decadal temperature, soil-moisture, or snow contrasts. Greenness change along this transect was therefore an elevation-dependent redistribution of the whole profile rather than uniform greening or a single boundary displacement, and no robust directional correspondence with decadal hydroclimate contrasts was detected under the present decadal analysis.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate landslide susceptibility assessment (LSA) is essential for regional disaster prevention and mitigation planning. Although machine learning and deep learning models have been widely adopted for LSA, their transferability across regions is often compromised by distributional discrepancies between source and target datasets. To address this challenge, we adapted the conditional domain adversarial network (CDAN) framework for cross-regional landslide susceptibility assessment to mitigate domain shift and improve cross-regional robustness. Two cross-regional transfer experiments were conducted to compare CDAN with conventional machine learning, direct-transfer deep learning, and representative domain adaptation methods. The results showed that CDAN achieved the highest mean Accuracy and F1 score among the benchmark models in both target domains, with Accuracy values of 0.77 and 0.80 and F1 scores of 0.78 and 0.80, respectively. The corresponding values of the area under the receiver operating characteristic curve (AUC) were 0.837 and 0.836, respectively. Neighborhood-based spatial information also improved the direct-transfer performance of conventional machine learning models compared with single-pixel representations. These results indicate that the CDAN-based framework can reduce the reliance on labeled target-domain samples and support county-level cross-regional landslide susceptibility assessment under domain shift.

Abstract (英文): This study examines ocean conditions and rainfall-related flood candidates during Severe Tropical Storm Maysak using multisource observations in the northwestern South China Sea and Guangxi, China. Regional daily mean wind speed, significant wave height and mixed-layer depth peaked around 4 July 2026. Daily mean sea surface temperature (SST) on 5 July was 1.12 °C below the maximum during the study period. A daily first frontal analysis south of Hainan showed reductions of 15.3% in mean SST gradient and 25.6% in the mean daily strong front area between paired 15-day windows. Gradient decreases persisted across the tested windows and smoothing settings. Photosynthetically available radiation decreased by 24.1%, and diffuse attenuation at 490 nm increased by 7.9% across their respective windows. The merged chlorophyll-a retrievals increased descriptively by approximately 5.0%; sensor differences and limited common observations prevent interpreting this estimate as a bio-optical response independently corroborated across sensors. Integrated Multisatellite Retrievals for the Global Precipitation Measurement mission (IMERG) indicated a maximum accumulation of approximately 702 mm over the prolonged 2–16 July rainfall episode, including subsequent weather systems. Water-change masks identified 126.5 km2 of potential new inundation candidates, including 1.5 km2 of same-pixel multisensor overlap. The analysis documents marine changes, prolonged rainfall and regional water-change candidates within their stated observation windows.

Abstract (英文): The difficulty in accurately determining channel Manning’s roughness has long constrained the accuracy of river flood simulation. At present, Manning’s roughness is mainly assigned using empirical approaches such as lookup tables and visual comparison, which are insufficient to represent the spatial heterogeneity of roughness in natural rivers. Remote sensing provides valuable data support for flood simulation in areas with sparse hydrological gauges. Remote-sensing observations are objective and can be acquired over large areas, but they are temporally discontinuous, whereas hydrodynamic models provide physically meaningful and temporally continuous simulations. These two information sources are therefore naturally complementary, providing a new avenue for Manning’s roughness inversion through remote sensing and hydrodynamic model coupling. To address these challenges, this study proposes a new remote sensing and hydrodynamic model coupling framework centered on roughness inversion. SAR-derived inundation extent is used as a spatial constraint, and an objective function together with particle swarm optimization (PSO) drives an iterative closed loop between the one-dimensional HEC-RAS model and the remote-sensing constraint to autonomously approach the optimal roughness parameters and improve the reliability of flood simulation. To bridge the scale mismatch between one-dimensional hydrodynamic outputs and two-dimensional SAR observations, the HEC-RAS cross-section geometry TIN structure is used to spatialize the simulated water surface, enabling direct comparison between simulated and SAR-observed inundation extents. The Le’an River in Wuyuan, Jiangxi Province, China, was selected as the test reach to evaluate the applicability and reliability of the framework under sparse-gauge conditions. The model was calibrated using the SAR-derived inundation extent, and simulated water levels were subsequently compared with rating-curve-derived reference levels. Under the SAR inundation constraint, the agreement between simulated and observed inundation extents improved, with IoU increasing from 0.763 under the initial parameter set to 0.789. Comparison with rating-curve-derived reference water levels further showed that the optimized Manning’s roughness parameters improved water-level simulation, reducing RMSE from 0.811 m to 0.594 m and increasing NSE from 0.950 to 0.973. These results demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed method when continuous hydrological observations are unavailable. The framework provides an automated remote-sensing-constrained approach for Manning’s roughness inversion in hydrodynamic models of small and medium-sized rivers with limited in situ hydrological data.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate hourly regional precipitation forecasting is essential for flood warning and weather-risk management, but station-scale global navigation satellite system (GNSS) precipitation models cannot fully describe the spatial evolution of rainfall between stations. This study proposes an hourly regional precipitation forecasting (HRPF) model that combines gridded GNSS-derived precipitable water vapor (PWV) and gridded precipitation with a convolutional long short-term memory (ConvLSTM) encoder–decoder network. Missing values in the hourly PWV and precipitation records from 69 GNSS-collocated meteorological stations in Taiwan Province during 2008–2012 were reconstructed using singular spectrum analysis and then resampled onto a 0.06° latitude–longitude grid using nearest-neighbor interpolation. The model uses PWV and precipitation fields from the preceding 1–6 h as two input channels and predicts the precipitation field one hour ahead. Lagged autocorrelation analysis showed strong spatiotemporal dependence in both precipitation and PWV fields, with coefficients above 0.86 and 0.85, respectively. In annual-scale external validation, the HRPF model maintained probability of detection (POD) values above 90%, false alarm rate (FAR) values below 55%, and threat score (TS) values of 46.22–46.58%. Seasonal external validation showed POD values of 88.8–97.8%, with the highest TS in summer (52.8–53.3%) owing to lower FAR values (45.8–46.4%). These results indicate that GNSS-derived PWV can provide useful spatial water-vapor information for one-hour-ahead regional precipitation forecasting, while model skill remains season-dependent over Taiwan’s complex terrain.

Abstract (英文): Reliable drought assessment requires spatially continuous drought information, whereas station-based drought indices are spatially discrete and many existing gridded products are available at relatively coarse spatial scales. A geographically weighted SPEI-related auxiliary variable, termed GeoSPEI-1, is constructed from neighboring station-based SPEI-1 values to incorporate neighboring-station drought information into drought index generation. GeoSPEI-1 is jointly used with multi-source remote sensing and reanalysis variables as input to a geographically weighted feature-learning model, termed GeoCSL, which combines convolutional neural network, self-attention, and long short-term memory components to process the multi-source inputs. Input-combination experiments showed that GeoSPEI-1 was the primary contributor among the evaluated input variables within GeoCSL, while the multi-source environmental variables provided additional complementary information. Ten-fold cross-validation using the 2001–2010 training samples yielded an RMSE of 0.546 and an MAE of 0.407 for GeoCSL against station-based SPEI-1. These results indicate that incorporating geographically weighted neighboring-station information together with multi-source environmental variables can support the generation of spatially continuous drought information and the use of GCDI-1 as a supplementary gridded product for regional drought monitoring and assessment in China.
