Abstract (英文): Deformation of glacier–bedrock systems provides evidence of slope adjustment before catastrophic failure. Seven ascending line-of-sight (LOS) intervals characterize temporal change, while the final pre-event ascending–descending pair (P4) provides east–west and vertical displacement estimates. P4 spans 24-day windows ending on 19 and 16 August 2026. Relative to the surrounding terrain, median eastward and vertical displacements are +24.85 and −19.03 mm within the sampled possible detachment mapped by the United Nations Satellite Centre (UNOSAT), compared with +41.05 and +33.12 mm in the neighboring LOS deformation area. Opposing vertical tendencies and local east–west contrasts indicate differential movement across the rock–ice system. A positive LOS cluster lies 1.393 km from the possible detachment boundary and retains 78.8% of its connected area at coherence ≥ 0.70. The ascending record shows a positive pre-event contrast, a negative event-spanning contrast and a mixed post-event field. Together, these observations underpin a geological model in which oblique sliding increases loading at neighboring ice–bedrock contacts, promotes uplift and westward deflection, and transfers loading toward a valley constriction. We interpret this sequence as south to north, counter-clockwise adjustment accompanying differential sliding. The model connects the measured deformation pattern to structural weakness, changing glacial support and topographic confinement, providing a geological explanation for pre-event rock–ice adjustment within the source to valley hazard cascade.

Abstract (英文): THz-SAR provides high-resolution imaging but is highly sensitive to trajectory perturbations, while video SAR further requires efficient reconstruction of consecutive sub-aperture frames from incomplete measurements. Under variable curvature motion, forward-model errors introduce geometric and phase inconsistencies that weaken the interframe correlation exploited by tensor recovery. This article develops an integrated radius-of-curvature change rate (RCRC)-guided low-tubal-rank recovery framework for sparse aperture THz video SAR. An RCRC-aware variable curvature trajectory operator models deterministic motion-dependent effects, a tensor nuclear-norm prior exploits interframe redundancy, and pulsewise unit modulus phase factors compensate for residual perturbations. The resulting joint problem is solved by alternating proximal-gradient, tensor singular value thresholding, and closed-form phase updates. Controlled point target simulations and measured data experiments are conducted at pulse retention ratios of 60%, 40%, and 30%. A single global slow-time mask is applied to the shared pulse stream before sub-aperture slicing, ensuring consistent pulse omissions across overlapping frames. Comparisons with three literature baselines evaluate the integrated method, while two internal ablations examine the effects of RCRC modeling and residual phase estimation within the adopted TNN-regularized formulation. The 40% case preserves the principal scattering structure and is treated as a candidate sparse observation operating point, whereas the degradation at 30% identifies a more severe robustness boundary. Because the sparse observations are generated retrospectively from full-rate measurements, the experiments validate reconstruction under missing pulses but do not demonstrate physical reductions in PRF, transmitted pulses, or collected raw data volume.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Under dense vegetation cover, deep networks for lithological classification must resolve small spectral differences between rock–vegetation mixtures. To address this issue, we propose the Dual-Branch Multi-Source Lithological Classification Network (DBMSL-Net), which uses Gaofen-5 Advanced Hyperspectral Imager (GF-5 AHSI) hyperspectral imagery, Phased Array type L-band Synthetic Aperture Radar-2 (PALSAR-2) data, and terrain data. Specifically, one branch encodes pixel spectra, and the other encodes spatial features from radar and terrain observations in the surrounding neighborhood. The spectral branch retains the overall spectral shape and local responses within intervals derived from absorption features in laboratory rock spectra. Laboratory pretraining initializes this branch, and source-domain class prototypes provide additional spectral references. A reliability gate adjusts the prototype contribution using target-pixel features and their similarity to the prototypes. The two branches are fused using weights that depend on the normalized difference vegetation index and pixel features. The core classifier, the differentiable binning residual module (DBRM), is trained jointly with both branches. It learns piecewise feature–class relationships through soft binning and refines class scores through successive residual updates. During inference, full-spectrum and feature-interval predictions are combined using class-specific weights. Neighborhood adjustment accounts for target extent. Across runs, boundary-aligned minimum-margin consensus is used for the spatially restricted target, whereas probability averaging is used for the extensive target. We evaluate DBMSL-Net in Lannitang, Yunnan, China, where quartz monzonite porphyry and quartz diorite porphyrite have similar mixed-pixel spectra and markedly different labeled sample sizes. On the within-area test subset, DBMSL-Net achieves a Target Macro F1 of 0.8301. Average accuracy, macro-averaged F1, and mean intersection over union reach 0.9388, 0.8780, and 0.7897, respectively. All four metrics exceed those of the nine comparison methods under the shared evaluation framework. Ablation experiments show that neither branch alone approaches the complete system (0.5727 and 0.7135, compared with 0.8301), and that the residual stage adds a measurable gain in both the initial and the complete configuration. The classification maps preserve the distributions of both the local and the extensive target and support lithological discrimination and field verification in this vegetated study area.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Conventional landslide susceptibility assessments rely primarily on static environmental factors, which provide limited information on actual slope movement. The additional predictive value of interferometric synthetic aperture radar (InSAR) deformation features, and the consistency of this value, remain insufficiently established. This study assessed their contribution to landslide susceptibility mapping in Luding County, Sichuan Province, China. Sixteen static environmental factors were combined with three features derived from small baseline subset InSAR (SBAS-InSAR): long-term mean deformation velocity (Vmean), velocity variability (Vstd), and velocity trend (Kv). Random forest (RF), XGBoost (XGB), LightGBM (LGBM), and CatBoost (CAT) models were evaluated using 30 repeated spatial holdout splits and paired ablation experiments. Adding Vmean yielded modest median paired AUC gains of 0.003–0.005 across the four models under the primary 10 km spatial validation scheme. XGB achieved the highest mean AUC (0.914) with the M1 feature set, comprising the static factors and Vmean. Adding Kv or Vstd did not yield consistent further gains. Out-of-sample Shapley additive explanations (OOS-SHAP) identified rainfall, the coefficient of variation in elevation (CV), distance to faults, and lithology as the leading predictors; Vmean also made an appreciable contribution. The main predictors exhibited nonlinear responses. Spatial SHAP mapping and Moran’s I analysis further revealed pronounced spatial variation and significant clustering in feature contributions. These findings indicate that long-term mean InSAR deformation velocity provides a consistent but modest complement to conventional static factors, primarily through local adjustments to predicted susceptibility and the identification of regional differences.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Hybrid radiative transfer model–machine learning (RTM–ML) approaches can incorporate physical constraints into vegetation-trait retrieval, but their advantage over empirical learning remains uncertain for very-high-resolution, limited-band observations of structurally heterogeneous forest canopies. We compared six empirical machine-learning regression algorithms with a hybrid PROSAIL–Gaussian Process Regression (GPR) approach for retrieving derived canopy chlorophyll content (CCC) from four-band UAV multispectral imagery of pedunculate oak (Quercus robur L.) stands under contrasting oak lace bug (Corythucha arcuata) pressure. CCC was derived from field-derived leaf chlorophyll content and UAV-estimated leaf area index. Data were collected across three sampling periods from 2021 to 2023, and models were evaluated using tree-grouped three-fold cross-validation to maintain independence between training and held-out trees. Empirical performance varied among algorithms, sites, and sampling periods. At the infested site, GPR provided the strongest performance in May (R2 = 0.61, RMSE = 27.04 µg cm−2, NRMSE = 10.87%), whereas Random Forest performed best in September. At the reference site, Random Forest, least-squares linear regression, and kernel ridge regression provided the strongest results depending on sampling period and performance metric, with R2 reaching 0.60. According to the primary cross-validated predictive metrics (R2, RMSE, and NRMSE), the tested PROSAIL–GPR approach generally showed lower predictive performance than the empirical models under the same tree-grouped evaluation framework. Empirical GPR maps resolved substantial within-stand spatial variation in derived CCC. Overall, incorporating PROSAIL-simulated information did not improve predictive performance under the tested four-band, crown-scale configuration. Rather than identifying a single cause, the results indicate that the benefit of physically constrained retrieval depends on compatibility among model representation and parameterization, canopy structure, spectral information, and available training data.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Hyperspectral anomaly detection (HAD) aims to identify Earth’s surface anomalies without prior knowledge. However, most existing unsupervised HAD methods rely on training data roughly partitioned into background and anomalies, suffering from severe class imbalance and yielding feature representations heavily tied to scene-specific distributions. In contrast, supervised hyperspectral classification networks can extract well-organized spectral–spatial representations from diverse land-cover types; yet, this valuable knowledge remains underutilized in HAD due to the absence of labeled anomaly samples. To circumvent this limitation, this paper proposes a novel knowledge transfer paradigm from classification tasks to anomaly detection. The proposed framework employs a pretrained convolutional enhanced Transformer (CET) classification network as the teacher model, transferring its rich spectral–spatial context encodings via feature-level distillation. Concurrently, by synergizing latent feature alignment, Gaussian mixture model (GMM) priors, and reconstruction objectives, the student network is equipped with compact background modeling and highly discriminative representation abilities, which provide robust support for feature-space anomaly detection. Extensive experiments on spaceborne and airborne hyperspectral datasets demonstrate that our method substantially improves detection accuracy against complex terrestrial backgrounds with fast inference, while exhibiting superior cross-scene generalization.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Small-object detection in UAV imagery remains challenging because repeated downsampling weakens fine structural cues, conventional feature pyramids are poorly aligned with small-object-dominated scale distributions, and fixed detection heads provide limited adaptation. To address these issues, a Hierarchical–Bidirectional–Dynamic Network (HBD-Net) is developed as a parameter-compact detector based on YOLOv11s. HEEM reinforces edge, contour, and texture information at multiple backbone stages using orthogonal depthwise strip convolutions, residual detail extraction, and zero-initialized fusion. PLSS replaces the conventional P3–P5 hierarchy with P2–P4 to emphasize high-resolution features, while P2-BiFPN introduces bidirectional propagation and learnable weighted fusion. An integrated Dynamic Head further refines features across scale, spatial, and task dimensions. On VisDrone, HBD-Net achieves 39.0% mAP50 and 22.6% mAP50–95, improving the YOLOv11s baseline by 6.9 and 4.3 percentage points, respectively, while reducing parameters from 9.4 M to 3.7 M. Ablation studies confirm the complementary effects of the components. On HIT-UAV, HBD-Net reaches 84.1% mAP50 and 57.0% mAP50–95, indicating effectiveness under infrared aerial imaging. These results demonstrate that coordinated structural enhancement, high-resolution multiscale fusion, and adaptive prediction can improve UAV small-object detection with a compact parameter footprint.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate aircraft-region segmentation in aerial infrared imagery can provide a useful scene-understanding component for image-assisted aviation inspection. However, target scale variation and ambiguous boundaries remain challenging in simulated aerial infrared scenes. To address these issues, this paper develops a bidirectional collaborative framework that couples global semantic fusion with edge optimization. The Semantics-guided Multi-scale Context Fusion branch uses adaptive multi-scale feature extraction and cross-stage calibration to produce a reliable initial segmentation, while the Edge-Sensitive Superpixel Collaborative Refinement branch improves boundary localization through semantic-guided superpixel consistency. Their interaction forms a bidirectional closed loop in which semantic context guides edge refinement and refined boundaries feed back to semantic learning. Experiments on AISD, a dedicated physics-based simulated aerial infrared dataset, achieve an 86.29% mPA, 83.35% mF1, and 76.20% mIoU. Relative to the strongest baseline, P-Mamba, the gains are 0.89 percentage points in mPA, 1.52 points in mF1, and 0.88 points in mIoU, and a sourcewise holdout comparison indicates consistent performance under unseen source content within the AISD simulation pipeline. These results concern aircraft-region segmentation rather than direct diagnosis of thermal defects.

Abstract (英文): Squinted multi-channel spaceborne synthetic aperture radar (SAR) offers wide-swath coverage and flexible observation geometry. However, imaging algorithms based on chirp scaling (CS) may broaden the range spectrum beyond the available sampling bandwidth, causing range spectrum aliasing and degrading the imaging quality of targets at the range edges of wide-swath scenes. To address this problem, this paper proposes a range-subband imaging method. First, a range spectrum broadening model is established to identify the main factors affecting spectral broadening. A subband division criterion is then derived to determine the minimum number of subbands required to avoid aliasing. Finally, a processing procedure comprising range-subband division, individual subband focusing, and coherent fusion is developed to recover the full-band range-focused signal. Point-target and distributed-target simulations verify that the proposed method suppresses range-spectrum aliasing and preserves full-band range resolution, while a broadside airborne experiment confirms that it introduces no appreciable image-quality degradation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Emerging remote sensing technologies increasingly support coastal observation through Earth observation (EO) derivatives such as satellite-derived shorelines, unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV)- and light detection and ranging (LiDAR)-based elevation models, coastal land-use/land-cover products, bathymetric grids, turbidity and chlorophyll-a indicators, habitat maps, flood extents, and artificial intelligence (AI)-generated segmentation outputs. These EO-derived products are central to shoreline monitoring, coastal vulnerability assessment, flood-response planning, marine ecosystem management, and land–sea administration. Yet their evidential value depends on transparent provenance: users must know which sensor, acquisition condition, preprocessing chain, algorithm, model version, validation dataset, and uncertainty estimate produced each coastal product. The review synthesises 156 eligible publications identified through a systematic review process. Of these, 89 studies containing sufficient implementation and methodological detail were selected for detailed analytical coding. Following the Preferred Reporting Items for Systematic Reviews and Meta-Analyses (PRISMA) protocol, the review analyses application domains, provenance capture modes, representation strategies, semantic web technologies, and interoperability practices. A coastal EO thematic refinement translates the review findings into provenance requirements for satellite, UAV, LiDAR, Internet of Things (IoT), open-water, and AI-enabled coastal observation workflows. The review identifies persistent gaps in feature-level lineage, alignment between the World Wide Web Consortium Provenance Ontology (PROV-O) and International Organization for Standardization (ISO) geospatial metadata standards, spatial semantics, AI workflow transparency, uncertainty traceability, and scalable querying. To address these limitations, the paper introduces GeoPROV, a lightweight semantic profile of PROV-O designed for spatially explicit, feature-level provenance. GeoPROV aligns PROV-O with ISO 19115/19157, GeoSPARQL, Shapes Constraint Language (SHACL), and SpatioTemporal Asset Catalogue (STAC)-compatible catalogue practices. The framework enables EO-derived coastal products to be traced from raw observation through preprocessing, AI/geoprocessing, validation, catalogue publication, and coastal-administrative decision-making. The resulting GeoPROV profile integrates PROV-O, GeoSPARQL, SHACL, ISO 19115/19157 lineage concepts, and STAC metadata within a lightweight semantic framework demonstrated through a coastal EO product workflow and machine-actionable provenance implementation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Ecological changes in mining areas are characterized by long-term dynamics, temporal stages, and spatial heterogeneity. Existing monitoring methods often face challenges in balancing multidimensional ecological condition characterization with spatially consistent analysis and have limited capability to identify complex disturbance–recovery trajectories. To address these issues, an Object-based Ecological Index-driven Framework (OEI-GTCD) is proposed to monitor ecological disturbance and recovery trajectories in mining areas. The framework uses the Mining-area Ecological Index (MEI) to characterize multidimensional ecological conditions and conducts analysis based on spatially consistent object units. On this basis, a Globally Optimized Time-series Change Detection method (GTCD) is proposed to improve the identification of complex ecological disturbance–recovery trajectories by integrating trend-change detection, abrupt-change detection, and segmentation optimization. Landsat imagery from 2000 to 2024 was used to characterize the spatiotemporal patterns of ecological disturbance and recovery across the Daye Mining Area, China. The findings suggest that MEI captures multiple dimensions of ecological conditions, while object-scale analysis characterizes ecological changes through overall object-level trajectories, supporting their spatial interpretation. OEI-GTCD achieved disturbance and recovery detection accuracies of 88.4% and 84.8%, respectively, representing improvements of 6.0 and 5.2 percentage points over OEI-LandTrendr. Ablation analyses further highlighted the contributions of abrupt-change detection and the improved segmentation criterion to event identification, as well as the role of local breakpoint refinement in temporal localization. From 2000 to 2024, stable and continuously improving ecological states together accounted for 82.07% of the area represented by the non-water objects used in the ecological analysis, while disturbance and recovery processes exhibited pronounced spatiotemporal heterogeneity. Overall, OEI-GTCD provides an integrated approach for monitoring ecological disturbance and recovery and characterizing their spatiotemporal patterns in mining areas.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The accuracy of 3D digitisation methods is strongly influenced by the surface properties of the observed object, particularly its texture, reflectance, and material structure. This is especially relevant to the systematic metrological analysis of ancient stone vessels undertaken by the Artifact Research Foundation, where high-resolution 3D models are increasingly used for geometric analysis, including through dedicated tools such as Petrie Scope. This study assesses the potential influence of crystalline granite texture on residual dispersion in Structure-from-Motion (SfM) photogrammetry and handheld laser-triangulation scanning using granite metrological reference objects measured under controlled conditions before and after application of a self-sublimating scanning spray. For the unsprayed cube, the pooled empirical 99% residual interval was −33.8 to +33.1 µm for photogrammetry and −49.9 to +54.5 µm for handheld scanning; for the unsprayed cylinder, the corresponding intervals were −51.6 to +30.7 µm and −76.3 to +55.3 µm. Sprayed reconstructions in the principal comparison exhibited lower residual dispersion and circularity RMSD. Repeated handheld-scanner acquisitions supported this trend, while supplementary SfM analyses demonstrated the influence of camera-network configuration and calibration treatment on surface-residual dispersion. By linking surface residuals to circularity and centre-offset descriptors, this study provides an empirical basis for interpreting geometric regularity in ancient stone vessels. The results for the tested granite surfaces and workflows highlight the need to distinguish measurement-induced deviations from genuine manufacturing irregularities when analysing polished stone artefacts and ancient vessels.

Abstract (英文): Unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV) remote sensing provides a flexible and efficient approach to early forest fire monitoring and warning. However, small fire spots and smoke plumes in UAV imagery vary substantially in texture, morphology, and spatial distribution across scales. Interactions between complex forest backgrounds and target features cause information loss during successive feature compression and misaligned spatial and semantic responses during cross-layer fusion. Limited onboard computing and storage further constrain model complexity. To address these challenges, we propose the lightweight feature alignment and multi-scale enhancement network (FAME-Net), which alleviates feature degradation and response mismatches during encoding and fusion while reducing computational redundancy in multi-scale prediction. At the feature encoding stage, the lightweight multi-scale enhanced backbone (LMEB) combines lightweight downsampling with multi-scale context modeling to reduce computational overhead and preserve fine details of small fire spots and weakly textured smoke. Its hierarchical features are fed into the alignment-aware multi-scale feature fusion neck (AMFF-Neck), which coordinates spatial and semantic responses across scales through cross-layer interactions, reducing fusion bias caused by scale discrepancies and background-induced coupling. The resulting features are processed by the lightweight shared convolution detection head (LSCD), which performs scale alignment, shared convolution, and learnable scale compensation for efficient multi-scale prediction. FAME-Net achieves mAP50 values of 71.5% and 65.3% on the integrated forest fire dataset and the Yuxi Wildfire dataset, respectively, with 1.7 M parameters and 3.9 GFLOPs, indicating a favorable accuracy–efficiency trade-off. On an NVIDIA Jetson Orin Nano Super (8 GB), it achieves inference speeds of 36.2 and 31.6 FPS on the two datasets, respectively, further supporting its potential for resource-constrained UAV deployment.

</details>

Abstract (英文): This study evaluates whether the Whittle–Matérn (WM) covariance representation improves pixel-wise SAR inversion over bare agricultural soil which can exhibit scale-dependent roughness. WM includes a smoothness parameter that controls covariance and roughness spectrum shape and has a Gaussian–Gamma scale-mixture interpretation. It was compared with exponential, Gaussian, and powered exponential (PE) covariance models within the same modified extended advanced integral equation model. Co-registered RADARSAT-2 and Sentinel-1 observations provided five channels for inversion. All experiments used identical observations, ancillary inputs, parameter bounds, objective function, and multi-start optimization settings. The analysis focused on the three co-polarized channels, for which covariance structure enters the scattering model directly. At a co-polarized residual tolerance of 2dB, WM produced model-consistent solutions for 425 of 439 retained pixels, compared with 384, 387, and 423 pixels for the exponential, Gaussian, and PE models, respectively. Its blind point ratio was 3.19%, versus 12.53%, 11.85%, and 3.64%, respectively. On the 342 pixels retrievable by all four models, WM gave the lowest mean absolute error for all channels except Sentinel-1 VH. The largest improvement was obtained for Sentinel-1 VV, where WM reduced RMSE to 0.501dB, compared with 0.820dB, 0.786dB, and 0.606dB for the exponential, Gaussian, and PE models. WM also yielded the most complete retrieval maps and scene mean soil moisture estimates closest to the available SMAP and station reference means. These results show that covariance representation is a material source of structural difference in unregularized multi-sensor SAR inversion.

</details>

Abstract (英文): One-bit quantization technology effectively simplifies the hardware architecture of synthetic aperture radar (SAR) and significantly reduces the volume of echo data, making it a pivotal research direction in spaceborne SAR systems. However, the high computational cost and latency associated with one-bit SAR signal reconstruction have limited the efficiency of downstream target detection applications. To address the demand for efficient ship target detection in one-bit SAR imagery, a label self-supervised ship detection training method on one-bit non-reconstructed SAR images is proposed in this paper, which enables generic deep learning models to perform detection directly in one-bit SAR images based on the chirp scaling algorithm (CSA) without signal recovery during inference. First, a training framework based on curriculum learning is designed, aiming to discriminate between real ship targets and false targets. Second, a one-bit reconstruction and training label self-supervised correction branch is incorporated into the training framework to autonomously generate false target labels and progressively guide the model to learn false target features. Third, a one-bit non-reconstructed image dataset is proposed to validate the trained model, which maps only reliable ship target annotations from full-precision echo imaging results. Finally, experiments conducted on real SAR data demonstrate that the detection model derived from the proposed training method can directly detect ship targets in the non-reconstructed images. Compared with the classic “reconstruction-and-detection” workflow, the trained model runs inference, bypassing the reconstruction process, and achieves a 20-fold improvement in efficiency without compromising detection accuracy. This method provides a practical solution for the rapid acquisition of target information in large-scale ship monitoring using one-bit SAR.

Abstract (英文): Monitoring soil total nitrogen (TN) status and spatial variation is essential for preventing land degradation and supporting precision agriculture. Remote sensing (RS) bands and their derived spectral indices are important covariates for soil TN mapping. However, optical RS-based soil mapping may be ineffective in fog-prone regions because water vapor interferes with spectral information. Here, multi-source earth observation data were acquired, including synthetic aperture radar (SAR), multispectral, and topographic data. Spectral indices, SAR-derived indices, and topographic indices related to soil TN were systematically developed. Subsequently, the PCC-VIF method was used to assess the importance of RS-based indices and select the optimal covariates, referred to as SST covariates. Finally, SST covariates and measured soil TN values were used as the independent and dependent variables, respectively, to construct an XGBoost-based soil TN mapping model for regions with persistent foggy cover. The results showed that: (1) SST covariates included Ela, TWI, SOS, VHplusVV, VHdiffVV, B2, and NDGI; (2) the proposed RS-based soil TN mapping model achieved satisfactory accuracy, with an R2 of 0.82 and an RPIQ of 3.03. Compared with models developed using traditional approaches, the R2 increased by approximately 0.16–0.52; and (3) areas with high soil TN content were mainly located in the southern part of the study area, whereas lower TN values occurred in the northern part. Soil TN content and its spatial distribution were mainly influenced by agricultural planting activities and microtopographic conditions. This study provides new insights into RS-based soil TN mapping in fog-prone regions. The robust soil TN mapping framework based on SST covariates and the XGBoost algorithm can support food security and help prevent environmental degradation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Existing environmentally adjusted development indices can assign similar scores to regions with contrasting environmental–social configurations, while provincial averages conceal subprovincial variation. We developed a Coupling-Embedded Sustainable Development Index (CESDI), which retains the Sustainable Development Index (SDI) as baseline performance while incorporating an adjustment for the joint balance and overall development level of the environmental and social subsystems. CESDI was applied to 30 Chinese provincial-level units from 2002 to 2022. Exploratory predictions for 337 Chinese cities were generated using MOSAIKS-inspired Landsat features, nighttime lights, and dual-penalty ridge regression. CESDI preserved the interprovincial SDI gradient (Pearson r = 0.870 to 0.995) while differentiating provinces with similar SDI values but contrasting joint environmental–social conditions. National mean CESDI increased from 0.248 in 2002 to 0.465 in 2017 and declined to 0.371 in 2022. Nested province-grouped cross-validation yielded R2 = 0.4471 and RMSE = 0.1388. Feature-block ablation showed that the dual-penalty model outperformed daytime-only, nighttime-light-only, and equal-penalty specifications. The predicted share of high and very high classes increased from 12.5% to 37.1%, while within-province heterogeneity rose in 21 of 26 multi-unit provinces. The framework integrates baseline sustainability performance with a CCD-defined balance-and-level adjustment and provides an exploratory spatial-screening layer for identifying within-province variation.

Abstract (英文): Old-growth forests (OGFs) are conservation priorities under the EU Biodiversity Strategy for 2030, but identifying relict stands in Mediterranean Pinus nigra (black pine) forests is challenging due to structural complexity. We evaluated a multisensor remote sensing framework to estimate an Old-Growth Index (OGI) in Cazorla Natural Park (Spain) using high-density Airborne Laser Scanning (ALS) data from the Spanish National Plan for Aerial Orthophotography (PNOA-LiDAR, 2024), RGB–NIR orthophotographs, and multispectral imagery (Sentinel-2 and WorldView-3). Four predictor sets (ALS metrics only; ALS plus canopy cover fraction (CCF); ALS + CCF + Sentinel-2; and ALS + CCF + WorldView-3) were evaluated across Multiple Linear Regression, Random Forest, and Support Vector Regression (SVR) using repeated nested cross-validation. SVR combined with the predictor set ALS + CCF + WorldView-3 achieved the highest nested R2 (0.79), outperforming Random Forest and Multiple Linear Regression. However, models relying solely on ALS (R2 = 0.71) or ALS with CCF (R2 = 0.77) yielded statistically comparable performance. Furthermore, integrating Sentinel-2 (R2 = 0.61–0.68) provided no clear gain over ALS-based models. These findings demonstrate that open-access national LiDAR programs provide sufficient 3D structural information to map stand maturity without requiring commercial satellite imagery. Continuous spatial predictions of OGI offer valuable baseline maps to support conservation planning and OGF monitoring in Mediterranean pine ecosystems.

</details>

Abstract (英文): High-resolution remote sensing images exhibit large variations in object scale, ambiguous boundary transitions, and complex interactions among adjacent land-cover regions. Under sparse pixel supervision, these factors may cause object fragmentation, erroneous merging, and boundary displacement. To address these issues, a Dual-Domain Coupled 2-Additive Fuzzy Measure Network (DC2CNet) is proposed for sparse pixel-supervised remote sensing image segmentation. The proposed method extends pixel-level class prediction by jointly modeling regional consistency and boundary discrimination. A region-domain branch and a boundary-domain branch are constructed to characterize intra-region coherence and class-transition information, respectively. A dual-domain coupling mechanism is further introduced to integrate regional and boundary evidence. In addition, a 2-additive fuzzy measure decoding strategy is developed to model individual evidence contributions and pairwise evidence interactions through Möbius representation. During training, sparse pixel annotations are used to generate boundary discrimination probabilities, and neighborhood consistency constraints are introduced to optimize the boundary branch. Experimental results demonstrate that DC2CNet effectively reduces boundary ambiguity and region confusion, while maintaining accurate segmentation of small objects, elongated structures, and complex interface regions.

</details>

Abstract (英文): This study presents a reliability-aware feedback-controlled fusion framework for hyperspectral image (HSI) and LiDAR classification under spatially disjoint conditions. Instead of committing to a fixed fusion configuration, the framework couples a library of fusion operators (geometry-aware fusion, adaptive gating, cross-modal transformer fusion and physics-informed fusion), integrated within a unified GhostConv-based architecture, with a closed-loop controller that evaluates candidate operators on validation data, accepts them only when they improve validation accuracy, and applies feedback-guided residual refinement. Experiments are conducted on Houston2013, Muufl, and Augsburg under a sample-center spatially disjoint protocol, in which training and validation samples are drawn from development blocks that are spatially disjoint from the test blocks, with seven labeled samples per class and ten random partitions. The framework achieves mean overall accuracies of 70.00%, 73.20%, and 66.34%, improving the Baseline-Ghost model on Houston2013, Muufl, and Augsburg; the improvement achieved by closed-loop training is preserved under a patch-disjoint protocol. Budget-matched analyses show that, with seven labels per class, the gain stems mainly from validation-gated closed-loop training and feedback-guided refinement, whereas the benefit of adaptive operator switching emerges as the label budget increases.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate monitoring of river water surface elevation (WSE) is important for hydrologic and flood applications, particularly where in situ observations are limited. This study evaluates Sentinel-1 Synthetic Aperture Radar (SAR) for estimating river WSE using a river inundation area–water surface elevation (RIA–WSE) relationship derived from high-resolution topobathymetric elevation data and investigates environmental factors affecting estimation accuracy. A total of 265 Sentinel-1 SAR images acquired during 2015–2026 covering the USGS Thebes gauging station along the Mississippi River were analyzed using six VV- and VH-based backscatter metrics, yielding 1590 WSE estimates. Comparison with a conventional 1-m digital elevation model (DEM) revealed that its representation of river water-surface elevations at the time of the elevation survey, rather than submerged channel-bed elevations, limits its applicability for estimating WSE at low river stages. Among the six metrics, log10(VV) produced the best overall performance, with an RMSE of 1.61 m, mean error of −0.96 m, R2 of 0.81, and percentage of lowest absolute error (PLE) of 44.5%. A sensitivity analysis using SAR image grid spacings of 10, 5, 2, and 1 m showed that log10(VV) maintained relatively stable estimation accuracy, whereas finer resampling did not consistently improve WSE estimates. Errors were smallest under low-flow conditions (WSE < 97.0 m), largest under medium- to high-flow conditions (97.0–102.5 m) and decreased again during flooding (WSE > 102.5 m). Analysis of 10 National Agricultural Imagery Program (NAIP) images revealed increasing vegetation along the inundation boundary above approximately 100 m WSE. Under medium- to high-flow conditions, calm conditions generally produced smaller errors than non-calm conditions, although errors did not increase monotonically with wind speed. E–W and SE–NW wind orientations generally produced larger errors than N–S and NE–SW orientations. These results indicate that WSE estimation accuracy reflects combined effects of vegetation, wind-generated water surface roughness, wind orientation, effective fetch, water depth, and river morphology. Although log10(VV) performed best overall, it provided the lowest absolute error for only 44.5% of acquisitions, highlighting the potential for condition-dependent backscatter metric selection to improve SAR-based river WSE retrieval.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Limited labeled data and class imbalance complicate polarimetric synthetic aperture radar (PolSAR) image classification. We propose a complex-valued multiscale feature interaction attention vision transformer (CV-MfIaViT) that combines spatial analytic-envelope preprocessing, complex-valued local feature extraction and global modeling. A two-dimensional Hilbert transform (2D HT) provides envelope features to the shallow pathway. The complex-valued amplitude–phase interaction attention (CV-APIA) module generates positive real gains through cross-modulation of pooled features; the final gating preserves the element-wise phase of non-zero input features. A complex-valued feature complementary mapper (CV-FCM), a complex-valued multiscale extractor (CV-MSE) and a complex-valued vision transformer (CV-ViT) complete the architecture. Under random-pixel sampling, the reported average accuracy (AA) exceeds CV-MsAtViT by 1.83, 3.42 and 2.48 percentage points on Flevoland, San Francisco and Nanjing, respectively. The advantage is also retained under spatial-block partitioning with shared label-free scene-level normalization. Component ablations and comparisons with two simple learned front ends characterize the combined design. The results support the evaluated architecture without isolating phase preservation as the cause of its accuracy gains.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The NASA–ISRO Synthetic Aperture Radar (NISAR) mission provides L-band soil moisture at 200 m, but validating its early products depends on retrieval flags, overpass collocation and sensor depth. We evaluated the beta Level-3 product against seven U.S. in situ networks (October 2025–January 2026) and the provisional product at 21 National Ecological Observatory Network sites (June–July 2026). Beta retrievals with flag 0, which averages all three candidate algorithms, were matched to in situ data within 60 min, merged by orbit and averaged over 0–10 cm sensors, yielding 261 site overpasses from 117 sites. Beta NISAR had a preliminary bias of 0.003 m3 m−3, a root mean square error (RMSE) of 0.081 m3 m−3 (95% interval 0.066–0.098) and R=0.578. Accepting all flag classes increased coverage ninefold and RMSE to 0.096 m3 m−3. On common site overpasses, deeper sensors did not have significantly higher RMSE than 5 cm sensors. The exploratory provisional assessment found a wet bias of 0.110 m3 m−3, concentrated at very dry reference sites, with a pooled R of −0.038 but an anomaly R within sites of 0.259. These estimates rest on few sites and short records; validation of early NISAR products should report flag classes, overpass construction and matched depths and separate pooled from temporal agreement.

Abstract (英文): Interferometric Synthetic Aperture Radar (InSAR) is a satellite–based remote sensing technique capable of measuring surface displacement. However, InSAR measurements can be affected by temporal decorrelation, baseline geometry, and surface characteristics. Persistent scatterer (PS) and small baseline subset (SBAS) are two widely used multi-temporal InSAR techniques for addressing these limitations. Processing SAR acquisitions to derive deformation maps and displacement time series requires a series of complex steps. Many existing open-source and commercial software packages support PS or SBAS, while some support both methods, each with its own challenges and limitations. In this study, we presnet SNAPxtra, a Python-based InSAR automatic workflow to generate PS and SBAS interferometric stacks. These can be exported in the format required for time-series analysis in LiCSBAS and StaMPS. SNAPxtra has automated master selection, multi-swath and multi-frame processing, polygon-based masking, trial processing, and network generation features within an integrated workflow. The reproducibility and adaptability of SNAPxtra are demonstrated through case studies in Quetta (Pakistan), Surat (India), and Fort Worth (USA) as case studies using both PS and SBAS methods.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate assessment of crop phenology under changing climate conditions is essential for agricultural planning and food security, particularly in regions where wheat production is sensitive to temperature and water availability. This study examines the long-term phenological responses of wheat across six sites in Greece and five in Cyprus over a 24-year period (2001–2024), using MODIS-derived NDVI time series to extract start of season (SOS), end of season (EOS) and length of season (LOS). SOS advanced consistently in both countries, while EOS was delayed in Greece but advanced in Cyprus. As a result, LOS increased across Greek sites but showed mixed responses in Cyprus, with no significant average change. Spatial differences were associated with latitude, with higher-latitude Greek sites exhibiting later SOS and EOS, whereas temporal trends were linked to climatic variability. Linear mixed-model analyses identified temperature as the dominant climatic driver of SOS and EOS in both regions, with precipitation exerting additional, but region-specific influences. A key finding is that atmospheric CO2 emerged as an additional statistical predictor of EOS in Greece, where it was associated with later EOS, offsetting the climate-driven advance in the model, and was marginally associated with LOS extension. In contrast, no CO2 association was detected for Cyprus, possibly reflecting more constrained environmental conditions. These findings highlight the importance of integrating geographic, climatic and atmospheric variables to capture crop-specific responses and support region-specific adaptation strategies in Mediterranean agroecosystems vulnerable to climate change.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Radar precipitation information with high spatial and temporal resolution is essential for flood forecasting, urban pluvial flood warning, and digital twin basins, yet radar quantitative precipitation estimation (QPE) is affected by observational errors, retrieval relationships, terrain effects, and multi-source merging. Based on 1328 Web of Science Core Collection publications (2000–2025), this study integrates bibliometric analysis, Transformer-based semantic representation, BERTopic modeling, semantic association analysis, and bibliographic coupling to map the topic structure, thematic evolution, and methodological lineages of research on radar precipitation uncertainty and hydrological applications. Nineteen micro-level topics were identified and grouped into four categories: measurement error identification and correction, uncertainty characterization and modeling, error propagation and scale effects, and operational applications and risk decision-making. The semantic structure shows that observation and correction and hydrological propagation constitute the main branches, while AI-driven forecasting is emerging. Methodological lineage analysis indicates that Statistical Fusion dominates the field, Physics provides a foundation, and AI and Hybrid methods have grown markedly in recent years, with Hybrid methods bridging physical mechanisms, statistical fusion, and data-driven approaches. Future research should integrate explainable AI, physical constraints, probabilistic modeling, and hydrological-response consistency testing to advance chain-wide uncertainty representation from observation and forecasting to hydrological propagation and risk-informed decision-making.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The consequences of drought for rivers depend not only on the atmospheric water deficit but also on how catchments store and release water. It remains unclear how well catchment characteristics distinguish streamflow drought occurrence under similar meteorological conditions. We combined AlphaEarth satellite descriptors with principal component analysis to examine this question in 361 relatively undisturbed catchments across the conterminous United States. Using the three-month Standardized Precipitation Evapotranspiration Index (SPEI) and Standardized Streamflow Index (SSI), we linked meteorological droughts to subsequent streamflow droughts and assessed whether catchment landscapes helped explain differences in their occurrence, intensity and duration under comparable meteorological and initial streamflow conditions. Occurrence was most clearly associated with PC1, the dominant component of the AlphaEarth descriptors, whose landscape differences were partly reflected in drainage density and forest cover. At comparable meteorological drought durations and the same meteorological intensity and initial streamflow conditions, catchments in the upper PC1 quartile had estimated occurrence probabilities 8.1–20.9 percentage points below those in the lower quartile. The separation was greater for moderate-or-stronger drought, at 13.8–22.3 points. Higher PC1 also tended to accompany lower drought intensity, but the evidence for intensity and duration differences was less conclusive than for occurrence. These findings support the use of a common satellite-derived landscape description to identify catchments more susceptible to streamflow drought under comparable meteorological conditions, while showing that occurrence probability alone cannot indicate how severe or persistent the resulting drought will be.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Satellite-based characterization of vegetation phenology in structurally complex Mediterranean ecosystems remains challenging because of the contrasting spatial and temporal properties of remote sensing platforms. This study evaluates how differences between sensors, including spatial and temporal resolution, influence phenological parameter extraction from NDVI time series in a cork oak woodland in central Portugal. NDVI time series from Landsat 8/9, Sentinel-2, and a ground-based spectrometer (FloX) were processed in TIMESAT to extract six phenological metrics across three vegetation types (cork oak, herbaceous vegetation, and open woodland) over three growing seasons (2022–2025). Landsat 8/9 and Sentinel-2 agreed closely for herbaceous vegetation and open woodland, whereas differences concentrated in cork oak, where Sentinel-2 estimated a start of season 161 days earlier in Year 1 and an end of season 33 to 61 days later in Years 2 and 3. Sentinel-2 retrieved higher amplitude and seasonal integral values in all vegetation types and years. Compared with FloX, satellite end of season and peak timing for cork oak were later in all years (by 72–198 and 87–203 days, respectively), whereas deviations for herbaceous vegetation were 15 days or less. These findings support multi-scale phenological characterization of Mediterranean montados and offer a baseline for future multisensor assessments of dry-land ecosystems.

Abstract (英文): Cross-sensor landslide mapping is limited by differences in image resolution and unreliable target pseudo-labels. We present SCALAR (Scale Anchoring and Label-Free Recalibration), an unsupervised adaptation workflow built on an exponential-moving-average teacher and ClassMix. GSD Anchoring (GSA) uses ground sampling distance metadata for bounded resampling. Logit-Otsu Calibration (LOC) jointly adjusts the pseudo-label boundary and relative retention margins. Ratio-Signed Regularization (RSR) makes these margins asymmetric according to the target-to-anchor GSD ratio. It is exploratory; GSA + LOC with symmetric margins is the recommended configuration. These modules introduce no trainable parameters. In the primary CAS region-held-out evaluation across five seeds, GSA + LOC increased the landslide intersection over union (IoU) by 17.9±4.5 points beyond scale anchoring when transferring satellite/aerial imagery (SAT) to unmanned aerial vehicle imagery (UAV), with no resolved additional gain in UAV-to-SAT. At seed 0, SAT-to-UAV threshold-independent average precision (AP) increased from 0.3484 for the anchored source to 0.4809 with GSA + LOC on held-out regions, compared with an increase from 0.6371 to 0.6495 under the original transductive protocol. Under that protocol, fixed-checkpoint region-cluster bootstrap supported GSA gains in both directions and LOC gains only in SAT-to-UAV; RSR’s 95% intervals included zero in both directions. UAV-to-SAT benefits varied across backbones, and negative transfer occurred in some tested settings. The findings support coordinating scale and pseudo-label decisions within CAS while identifying teacher reliability and target resolution as conditions to examine before broader deployment.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Flood monitoring in Andean–Amazonian valleys is constrained by sparse hydrological records and persistent cloud cover. This study mapped observed flood recurrence in the Utcubamba River Valley, Peru, during 2019–2025 using Sentinel-1 imagery processed in Google Earth Engine. PRE–POST backscatter differences were classified using Otsu thresholding and spatial constraints, including a maximum slope of 5° and a 1000 m hydrographic buffer. Eight of 13 cataloged events were retained. VH thresholds had a mean ± standard deviation of −2.83 ± 0.05 dB, compared with −1.73 ± 1.11 dB for VV and −3.33 ± 1.44 dB for (VH+VV)2; the low VH dispersion is a property of the retained subset, which was selected partly on VH threshold behavior. The eight-event map covered 9842.02 ha detected as flooded at least once, with 6.29% classified as high or very high observed recurrence, concentrated near the Utcubamba–Marañón confluence. For E13, assessment against a balanced sample of 1360 reference points yielded a sample-based overall accuracy of 85.15%, flooded-class user’s accuracy of 91.64%, producer’s accuracy of 77.35%, and F1 score of 0.839. These metrics characterize the sampled sectors of E13. Agricultural classes accounted for 73.70% of the area in the 2024 land-cover overlay. Irrigation-related changes could not be fully distinguished from river flooding. The resulting count of detections across cataloged events provides complementary information for land-use planning, with interpretation bounded by temporal coverage, event selection, and reference-data availability.

Abstract (英文): Stratospheric ozone exhibits quasi-biennial-range variability, whose spatial organization can differ among pressure levels and longitudes. We analyzed Aqua Atmospheric Infrared Sounder (AIRS) Version 7 monthly ozone observations from September 2002 to December 2025 at 50, 10, and 3 hPa. A Morlet wavelet analysis of standardized anomalies characterized the 20–36-month variability, with AR(1) Monte Carlo significance tests and complementary multiple-testing and uncertainty assessments. The tropical 10 hPa band showed relatively enhanced normalized power and widespread longitudinal support: 326 of 359 eligible longitudes remained significant after a within-level Benjamini–Hochberg adjustment of band-level tests. In the global 10 hPa grid-cell analysis, 6792 of 62,191 eligible cells passed the corresponding global Benjamini–Hochberg adjustment. Conditional block-bootstrap comparisons indicated differences in some predefined longitude-sector contrasts, whereas the confidence intervals for the 10-minus-50 hPa longitude-power coefficient-of-variation difference included zero. Regressions using NOAA CPC and KIT/FU Berlin QBO wind records indicated longitude-sector heterogeneity in the 10 hPa ozone–QBO regression associations using the 50 hPa wind index. The ERA5 temperature and low-wavenumber geopotential height patterns provided meteorological context, although the assessed ozone–ERA5 spatial alignments did not pass the adopted spatial-null tests. These findings offer a pressure-dependent, longitude-resolved description of quasi-biennial-range ozone variability and selected QBO-associated statistical relationships, without establishing a unique dynamical or chemical attribution.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Data from terrestrial GNSS receivers have been combined with point clouds from aerial lidar to estimate the average attenuation coefficients of foliage in planted pine forests at GNSS frequencies. The absorption and scattering of radio signals due to the presence of vegetation can degrade signals from communications and satellite-based navigation systems, making accurate estimates of attenuation essential for predicting equipment performance. Given the potentially limited ability of lidar to penetrate dense foliage, a method was developed to estimate the average attenuation of signals based on distance travelled beneath the maximum height envelope of tree canopies. This was accomplished by using aerial lidar data to determine the outer surface of the forest canopy based on the maximum height in each horizontal bin. Each GNSS satellite detection vector was then analysed to determine the total length traversed inside of the canopy envelope. Application to regions of planted radiata pine in a managed forest outside Adelaide, South Australia, yielded average attenuation coefficient estimates for satellites with a 30–60° elevation angle of 0.36 dB m−1 for 10-year-old medium-height and 0.35 dB m−1 for 19-year-old tall trees. The demonstrated method and results provide site and vegetation-type-specific data to inform predictive models of GNSS receiver performance at specific locations.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Remote-sensing-derived static terrain and land-cover datasets influence regional extreme heat simulations, but their effects on internal model responses and temperature performance require joint assessment. Daytime sensible heat flux difference fields had spatial root-mean-square (RMS) values exceeding 20 W/m2. On the WRF Preprocessing System (WPS)-mapped 3 km grid, terrain fields derived from ASTER30 and GMTED5m had mean absolute differences of 7.5 and 22.8 m, respectively, relative to GMTED30s. Terrain-height and surface-pressure differences were strongly inversely correlated (R = −0.9990 to −0.9993), supporting hydrostatic consistency. Direct interaction residuals indicated non-additive TSK and T2 responses, and their full-period mean fields had spatial RMS values of 0.421 and 0.225 K, respectively. All configurations exhibited pooled TSK warm biases of 5.52–5.74 K, compared with an inter-configuration bias spread of approximately 0.22 K. No configuration consistently minimized temperature errors across variables and periods, and larger internal response magnitudes did not consistently correspond to smaller temperature errors. For this event and model configuration, products with higher nominal native resolution did not consistently improve temperature simulation; the independent resolution effect was not isolated. The framework combines internal diagnostics with station and satellite evaluation to assess remote-sensing-derived static inputs.

Abstract (英文): Tropospheric phase delay is a major error source that limits the accuracy of Interferometric Synthetic Aperture Radar (InSAR) deformation measurements, including those from the C- and X-band systems commonly examined in the studies reviewed here. Traditional correction methods, including external atmospheric models, spatiotemporal filtering, and phase–elevation empirical approaches, can partially mitigate atmospheric delays but often suffer from limited resolution, strong assumptions, or difficulties in separating atmospheric signals from complex surface deformation. Recent advances in deep learning provide new opportunities for InSAR tropospheric correction by learning nonlinear spatial and temporal relationships from interferometric observations and auxiliary information. This review provides a focused overview of deep learning-based approaches for correcting tropospheric phase delays in InSAR. Existing studies are organized according to their dominant modeling strategies, including convolutional neural network (CNN)-based spatial modeling, recurrent neural network (RNN)-based temporal modeling, generative adversarial network (GAN)-based generative learning, and other feedforward regression approaches. Key network-design strategies, training-label and dataset construction methods, and representative applications are discussed. Finally, current limitations and future research directions are discussed, emphasizing physical consistency, deformation preservation, cross-region generalization, and independent geodetic validation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Smoke from the Canadian wildfires of August 2025 reached Cluj-Napoca, Romania, on 6–9 August as a thin layer at 5–8 km. Along the air mass trajectory, the HARP2 retrievals resolved the ageing of the smoke: an optical depth decaying from 0.9 to 0.2–0.3, a layer rising to 10 km and descending to 5 km, growth of the fine-mode radius, and a single-scattering albedo consistent with brown carbon loss. At the site, the lidar ratio and particle depolarisation ratio retrieved by HARP2 (52 sr, 0.042) agreed with the lidar’s derived lidar ratio and measured depolarisation (50 sr, 0.042), whereas the three satellite optical depths differed by 0.2 around an AERONET value of 0.1 and the NATALI typing networks returned three different classes. For this faint, aged plume, intensive polarimetric and vertical quantities could no longer be identified for the aerosol by its optical depth and Ångström exponent, which motivates testing an intensive-first pairing of satellite polarimetry with ground-based lidar on further episodes.

Abstract (英文): Optical and synthetic aperture radar (SAR) image matching is fundamental to numerous remote sensing applications. Although phase congruency-based methods have been widely adopted for this task, the structural features they extract remain inherently sensitive to speckle noise, which significantly degrades matching performance. To address this limitation, we propose a novel noise-robust multiscale structural feature (NMSF) method. Unlike existing relative total variation (RTV)-based approaches that modify the RTV model or rely on phase congruency, our framework employs the standard RTV model with a principled exponential scaling scheme to construct multiscale structural maps (MSM), effectively capturing salient structural information across multiple scales while suppressing noise and mitigating modality discrepancies. Building upon the MSM, we develop an MSM-ORB detector with non-maximal suppression to extract reliable and well-distributed keypoints. We further introduce a multilayer features of oriented gradient (MFOG) descriptor with a square grid nested architecture, which fundamentally differs from the log-polar structures used in prior methods and achieves multiscale integration without dimensional inflation. Extensive experiments on publicly available datasets demonstrate that NMSF consistently outperforms state-of-the-art approaches, delivering superior robustness and accuracy under nonlinear intensity variations, geometric distortions, and severe speckle noise.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Historic hill towns require geometric information capable of relating urban morphology, construction features, and structural details within a common spatial and semantic framework. This paper presents a multi-sensor, multi-scale geomatics workflow for establishing an integrated geospatial information framework for the historic centre of Fiumefreddo Bruzio in Calabria, southern Italy. A control network of 38 points provided the spatial reference for UAV and terrestrial photogrammetry, six SLAM trajectories, and 270 static laser scans covering five buildings. Point clouds generated by Structure from Motion and range-based sensors were co-registered in the RDN2008/UTM zone 33N coordinate reference system and used to produce orthophotos, digital terrain and surface models, contour lines, textured meshes, and building-scale drawings. These products were organised within an interoperable geospatial information system based on persistent links between settlement-, aggregate-, building-, façade-, space-, and macroelement-level entities. The framework also supported structural characterisation and seismic vulnerability assessment at building and urban scales, linking building screening and potential local mechanisms to their spatial interpretation. The resulting survey-based digital model supports conservation documentation, seismic screening, and street-network analysis, while providing the foundation for future digital twin development rather than an operational implementation. The study offers a transferable acquisition-to-information workflow, preserving data provenance and metric quality for risk-informed management of historic settlements.

Abstract (英文): Enhancing the environmental generalization of Synthetic Aperture Radar (SAR) Automatic Target Recognition (ATR) requires diverse target–clutter combinations. Despite abundant target-free clutter, exhaustively capturing multi-background target samples is prohibitive. However, existing generative methods either distort authentic clutter statistics through full-scene reconstruction or lack autonomous generation capabilities by strictly relying on pre-existing target instances. To overcome these limitations, we propose the Masked Inpainting and Residual Blending Network (MIRB-Net) for instance-free, background-preserving, multi-class coupled generation directly onto authentic measured backgrounds. Treating the background as a measured clutter carrier, MIRB-Net synthesizes class-conditional targets in situ using three synergistic mechanisms. First, a Background-Preserving Residual Editing (BPRE) mechanism explicitly decouples spatial blending from residual prediction, effectively confining generative perturbations to the target vicinity. Second, a self-supervised Masked Inpainting Pretraining (MIP) strategy specifically designed for BPRE equips the network with crucial priors of target scattering and background statistics. Third, joint global–local adversarial supervision alongside tri-region spatial constraints ensures seamless radiometric coupling across target–clutter boundaries. Evaluated under a dedicated multidimensional assessment framework on the MSTAR dataset, MIRB-Net demonstrates superior local structural fidelity, faithful background preservation, and natural target–clutter coupling. Furthermore, hybrid data-augmentation experiments confirm that the autonomously generated samples consistently improve downstream ATR performance across diverse deep learning architectures.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV) optical remote sensing provides a flexible means of mapping and inspecting power-transmission corridors. However, the automatic extraction of power lines from aerial imagery remains challenging because conductors have slender geometry, weak texture, low contrast, and discontinuous visibility and are easily affected by imaging noise, occlusion, and complex background interference. Consequently, segmentation-based methods often produce fragmented masks that limit the reliability of subsequent structural reconstruction. This study presents a power line detection and reconstruction framework based on global keypoint regression for UAV optical imagery. Rather than recursively associating adjacent keypoints, the framework regresses every keypoint belonging to the same power line toward a common start point. This shared reference is designed to avoid recursive propagation through a chain of local associations. Three complementary components are incorporated: a multi-scale region-aware feature extractor (MSRAFE) for combining local and global semantics across scales, a line feature aggregation (LFA) module for adaptive multi-level feature fusion, and a linear structure-aware feature strategy (LSAFS) for coupling local geometric cues with global structural relationships. The method was evaluated on two public aerial-image benchmarks and a self-constructed dataset. It achieved intersection-over-union (IoU) and F1 scores of 85.76% and 91.76% on PLDU and 75.23% and 83.95% on TTPLA, respectively. On the self-constructed CPLD dataset, it obtained the highest IoU (70.44%) and precision (83.87%) among the compared methods. These pixel-level results support the performance of the complete framework for detecting thin linear objects in airborne optical remote sensing imagery.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Deep-learning research on flood inundation mapping is driven largely by architectural innovation and single-run reporting. We ask whether the segmentation architecture or the input representation contributes more to accuracy in flood-event surface-water mapping. We introduce GeoHybridNet, a hybrid CNN–Transformer network, and compare it on the hand-labeled Sen1Floods11 benchmark with a U-Net, an Attention U-Net, a parameter-matched wide U-Net, and SegFormer under matched data and training. The four convolutional and hybrid models differ by at most 1.4 IoU points, and none are significantly different under event-level tests; a compact SegFormer trained from scratch is about 3.5 points lower. In factorial designs crossing three architectures with up to five input representations (five seeds each), input representation explains about 95% of the variance in water IoU and architecture about 1%. The dominant input factor is radiometric normalization: raw bands standardized per chip reach only 60% IoU and adding NDWI restores 76%, whereas the same bands standardized with training-set statistics reach 77.5% without any index. The pattern holds when permanent water is excluded. On three held-out flood events, accuracy drops by 24–26 IoU points for every model. On this benchmark, input preparation and honest evaluation matter more than architecture.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate forest-cover mapping in fragmented Mediterranean landscapes remains challenging because forests, croplands, grasslands, and sparsely vegetated surfaces often exhibit overlapping spectral responses. This study benchmarks ten supervised machine-learning classifiers for regional forest-cover mapping in the Beni Mellal-Khenifra region of Morocco, evaluates the contribution of spectral, textural, and topographic predictors, and assesses the consistency of the resulting forest map with the Hansen Global Forest Change product. A reference dataset of 2800 samples representing forest, crops/grass, bare ground, urban areas, and water was combined with an annual 2025 Sentinel-2 median composite, spectral indices, NDVI-derived gray-level co-occurrence matrix textures, and SRTM-derived elevation and slope. The samples were divided once into a stratified 70% training set and a 30% test set common to all classifiers. Redundancy, correlation (|r| > 0.80), and variance inflation factor (VIF < 10) screening, fitted on the training set only, reduced 56 candidate predictors to 16, and hyperparameters were tuned by five-fold grid-search cross-validation within the training set. On the common test set (n = 840), CatBoost, XGBoost, and the multilayer perceptron each achieved an overall accuracy of 95.95% (Cohen’s κ = 0.947), followed by Gradient Tree Boosting (95.83%); McNemar tests with Holm correction detected no significant differences among these four models. Spatial block cross-validation reduced overall accuracy by 0.3–2.7 percentage points, and CatBoost retained the highest spatially blocked accuracy (95.51% with 10 km blocks). SHapley Additive exPlanations (SHAP) identified slope, SATVI, NDWI, and the blue band as the dominant contributors to CatBoost forest predictions. The leading maps estimated 859,057–870,663 ha of forest. CatBoost captured 90.9% of the Hansen forest extent (≥30% canopy cover) but mapped a much larger forest area; blind interpretation of stratified samples showed that 90.5% of the CatBoost-only forest was woody vegetation, mainly open woodland and matorral. These findings show that boosting ensembles combined with a compact, non-redundant predictor set provide accurate and spatially robust forest-cover maps and that differences from global products largely reflect forest definitions.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate target prior information is an important basis for improving the detection and recognition performance of high-resolution range profiles (HRRPs). To address the insufficient reliability of prior templates in scattering-center-assisted HRRP target recognition based on SAR images, where scattering centers extracted from a single image are susceptible to noise, sidelobes, local scattering fluctuations, and variations in observation azimuth angle, this paper proposes an HRRP target recognition method based on the fusion of scattering-center priors derived from SAR images acquired at adjacent azimuth angles. Two-dimensional scattering centers at different azimuth angles are extracted by combining an adaptive point-spread function (PSF) with the two-dimensional CLEAN algorithm. Amplitude-weighted clustering is then employed to retain stable scattering structures with spatial consistency, thereby constructing a fused scattering center model. Subsequently, the fused model is projected onto the range dimension under different azimuth offsets to establish a one-dimensional scattering center prior template library. Target classification is finally performed using sliding matching based on the maximum correlation coefficient. Same-band, cross-band, and comparative experiments conducted with ground-target data sets demonstrate that the proposed method effectively improves the stability of the prior templates and target recognition performance, while exhibiting reasonable adaptability to variations in observation azimuth angle, noise level, and radar operating frequency band.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Civil Air Patrol (CAP) imagery, often the first post-disaster observation, is designed for 2D interpretation rather than multi-temporal volumetric measurement. We build post-storm digital surface and terrain models from uncontrolled CAP imagery with structure-from-motion in western North Carolina’s open Swannanoa Valley and forested Hickory Nut Gorge. Surfaces are co-registered to pre-event LiDAR using roads as pseudo ground control, corrected for terrain-correlated bias, and thresholded with a spatially variable, calibrated Level of Detection. Before bias correction, bare-earth surfaces match withheld post-event LiDAR on stable terrain to 0.68 and 0.82 m normalized median absolute deviation. Volumes are 2017-to-2024 change, lower bounds in coverage, and conditional on the bias model, residual screen, and detection window. Corrected valley surfaces record 125,000 m3 of erosion and 123,000 m3 of deposition, and a sampled image check dates about 13% of that change to the sortie. Under the same limit, the uncorrected surface reports five times the deposition, and the alternative bias model gives gross terms 3.6 to 4.2 times larger. The gorge at Chimney Rock recorded 76,000 m3 of erosion and 84,000 m3 of positive change, just under half dating to the sortie, including land deposition partly cleared before the post-event LiDAR flew. CAP-derived surfaces can capture transient storage that later surveys cannot.

Abstract (英文): Individual-tree segmentation of dense point clouds increasingly supplies the crown dimensions from which crown–stem allometry is fitted, yet no study has measured how much the fitted crown–stem exponent changes with what is done to material a segment holds but may not own. In two 1 ha plots spanning the open-to-closed canopy gradient of a Southeast Asian tropical dry forest, scanned with handheld SLAM LiDAR against a field stem map of 1320 trees, we voxelized each of 340 segments at 5 cm, split it into 26-connected components and computed crown projected area three times with identical code: on the delivered segment, after removing every component not physically continuous with the tree’s own bole (strict) and after removing only those components in which a field-mapped stem other than this tree falls inside the component’s own footprint (conservative). In the closed evergreen stand the strict treatment moved the exponent of crown area on stem diameter from 1.54 to 1.04 (paired-bootstrap difference −0.49, 95% CI −0.65 to −0.34). The conservative treatment moved it by −0.003 (−0.005 to −0.001), and its median removal was 0.000 of a segment’s points in both stands. Removal based on stem-footprint overlap therefore moves the exponent by two orders of magnitude less than removal of every disconnected component, a contrast that bounds the sensitivity without establishing the ownership of either group. The result holds with the crown base fixed at its delivered value, with the largest segments removed and on the segments whose crown base came from the primary detection rule (−0.372, −0.587 to −0.147). Its direction also holds across bole gates, crown-base thresholds, an alpha-shape crown area and connectivity voxels of 3 to 10 cm, although its magnitude scales with the voxel (−0.89 to −0.24). The crown–stem exponent obtained from segmented closed-canopy point clouds is therefore not determined by the point cloud alone, and we quantify how much of it the analyst decides.

Abstract (英文): Hyperspectral anomaly detection (HAD) requires effective background modeling while limiting the reconstruction of rare anomalous spectra. Conventional non-negative matrix factorization provides an interpretable endmember–abundance representation but offers limited flexibility for estimating background representations under complex spectral variability, whereas reconstruction-oriented autoencoders provide flexible nonlinear inference but often lack an explicit mixing-model structure. To bridge these two paradigms, we propose a Non-negative Latent Factorization Autoencoder (NLFA) for anomaly-oriented background modeling. NLFA employs a nonlinear encoder to estimate simplex-constrained abundance-like latent coefficients and a linear mixing model (LMM)-consistent linear decoder to represent the dominant background through endmember-like spectral bases. A column-sparse residual is jointly optimized with the latent factorization so that spectral components insufficiently explained by the learned background are retained for anomaly detection, while local spatial smoothness and endmember-diversity constraints regularize the background representation. Experiments on eight benchmark hyperspectral datasets show that NLFA achieves competitive detection performance.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Huanglongbing (HLB) is the most destructive disease affecting citrus production worldwide, causing severe yield losses and tree decline. Hyperspectral proximal sensing provides detailed information on leaf reflectance; however, its effectiveness for reliable HLB detection depends on the identification of consistent and discriminative spectral patterns. In this study, hyperspectral reflectance data (400–2450 nm) were combined with supervised machine learning algorithms to distinguish HLB-positive and HLB-negative citrus samples at two complementary scales: (i) leaf-level classification using individual spectral signatures and (ii) plant-level classification based on aggregated spectral responses. Model performance was evaluated under a leave-one-field-out validation framework designed to minimize spatial dependence between training and testing data. Results demonstrated consistently high classification performance, with Logistic Regression achieving accuracies above 0.94 at the leaf level and up to 1.00 at the plant level in several configurations. Dimensionality reduction using Principal Component Analysis (PCA) preserved most of the discriminative information, although with a slight reduction in recall under certain FIELD combinations. In contrast, Class balancing produced no observed improvement, whereas hyperparameter optimization showed limited and configuration-dependent effects in classification performance. Spectral coefficient analysis revealed that the most relevant wavelengths are concentrated in the visible (400–700 nm), red-edge (700–750 nm), near-infrared (750–1300 nm), and shortwave infrared (1500–2500 nm) regions, reflecting physiological alterations associated with pigment degradation, mesophyll disruption, and changes in water and biochemical composition. Overall, hyperspectral reflectance combined with parsimonious machine learning models enabled consistent discrimination of HLB-positive and HLB-negative samples within the evaluated orchard under validation designed to reduce spatial dependence, particularly at the plant scale.

</details>
